) with
(
8.4
), we obtain

\left|v^{\mathrm{T}}(G-\Phi)w\right|\leq C\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{|z|^{2}}+C\frac{n^{-500}}{|z|}.

Since
R_{\max}\leq n^{100}
and
\sigma_{\max}^{-1}\leq n^{100}
, we have
\mathfrak{m}_{D}\geq cn^{-100}
for an absolute constant
c>0
. Absorbing the
n^{-500}
-term, we get

\left|v^{\mathrm{T}}(G-\Phi)w\right|\leq\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{|z|^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

9
Proofs of the outlier eigenvalue locations: Theorems
5
and
6

9.1
Proof of Theorem
5

For notational convenience throughout this section, we replace the target index
k
with
j
. We first consider the case that
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
for
j\in[r_{+}]
is a simple eigenvalue. In this section, we use the normalized version

\alpha_{j}(z):=\lambda_{j}^{-1}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j},

so that
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(z)=\lambda_{j}\alpha_{j}(z)
. The deterministic
root
\vartheta_{j}
defined in Proposition
5.1
is equivalently characterized by
\alpha_{j}(\vartheta_{j})=0
.

We still work on the event
\mathcal{E}_{D}
. However, we only need a weaker signal assumption
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}+100\rho_{j}
since the local law is applied on deterministic contours around
\lambda_{j}
, whereas in the eigenspace proof, it is applied at the random points
z=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}.

By Proposition
5.1
, there exists a unique deterministic location
\vartheta_{j}
satisfying

1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\vartheta_{j})u_{j}=0

with

\left|\vartheta_{j}-\lambda_{j}-\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}\right|\leq 145\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{3}}.

In particular, since
\mathcal{V}_{jj}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\vartheta_{j}\leq\lambda_{j}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}+145\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{3}}\leq\lambda_{j}+\frac{1}{36}\lambda_{j}+\frac{145}{36^{2}}\lambda_{j}<1.16\lambda_{j}

(9.1)

and directly by Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\vartheta_{j}\geq\lambda_{j}

(9.2)

We will show that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{D}
, there exists exactly one eigenvalue
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
lying inside the disk

\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho):=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho\}

for
\rho\equiv\rho_{j}
defined in (
9.4
). Denote

\displaystyle K(z):=\Lambda^{-1}-U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U\quad\text{and}\quad B(z):=U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))U.

Set

\displaystyle g(z):=\det(K(z))\quad\text{and}\quad f(z)=\det(\Lambda^{-1}-U^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)U)=\det(K(z)-B(z)).

(9.3)

By Lemma 21 from
[
73
]
, the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{A}
outside the
[-\|E\|,\|E\|]
are the zeros of
f(z)
. Besides, the algebraic multiplicity of each eigenvalue matches the corresponding multiplicity of each zero. Next, we show that the zeros of
f(z)
are close to the zeros of
g(z)
.

The following result is a special case of the generalized Rouché’s theorem for operator functions (see
[
54
]
). We include a short proof for completeness.

L
EMMA
9.1
.

Let
\Gamma\subseteq\mathbb{C}
be a simple closed contour. Assume
K(z)
and
B(z)
are analytic in a neighborhood of
\Gamma
and its interior. If

\sup_{z\in\Gamma}\|K^{-1}(z)B(z)\|<1,

then
f
and
g
have the same number of zeros inside
\Gamma
, counting multiplicity.

Proof.

For
t\in[0,1]
, define
F_{t}(z):=\det(K(z)-tB(z))
. For
z\in\Gamma
,

K(z)-tB(z)=K(z)(I-tK^{-1}(z)B(z)).

Since
\|tK^{-1}(z)B(z)\|<1
,
I-tK^{-1}(z)B(z)
is invertible. Hence,
F_{t}(z)\neq 0
on
\Gamma
for all
t\in[0,1].
By the argument principle (
[
5
, Section 5.2]
), the number of zeros of
F_{t}
inside
\Gamma
is a constant in
t
. Taking
t=0
and
t=1
proves the claim.
∎

We will take a circle

\Gamma_{j}(\rho):=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-\vartheta_{j}|=\rho\}

with the radius

\displaystyle\rho\equiv\rho_{j}:=10M+15\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\lambda_{j}}.

(9.4)

To apply Lemma
9.1
on
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
, we bound

\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|K^{-1}(z)B(z)\|\leq\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|K^{-1}(z)\|\cdot\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|B(z)\|.

To bound
\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|B(z)\|
, first note that
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
. To see this, since
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}+100\rho_{j}
and

\rho_{j}\leq\lambda_{j}/100
, for every

z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho_{j})
,

\displaystyle|z|\geq\vartheta_{j}-\rho_{j}\geq\lambda_{j}-\rho_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}},

\displaystyle|z|\leq\vartheta_{j}+\rho_{j}\leq 1.16\lambda_{j}+\frac{\lambda_{j}}{100}\leq 2R_{\max}^{3}

Hence, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{D}
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|B(z)\|\leq\left(\frac{100}{99}\right)^{2}\frac{M}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.5)

To bound
\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|K^{-1}(z)\|
, we rewrite
K(z)
for
z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
in block form. Without loss of generality, by relabeling the columns of
U
and the diagonal entries of
\Lambda
, we may assume that the index
j
is the first one. Namely, write

U=(u_{j},U_{-j})\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{j}&0\\
0&\Lambda_{-j}\end{pmatrix}.

Thus,

K(z)=\Lambda^{-1}-U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{j}(z)&-b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}\\
-b_{j}(z)&D_{j}(z)\end{pmatrix},

where

\alpha_{j}(z):=\lambda_{j}^{-1}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j},\quad b_{j}(z):=U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j},\quad D_{j}(z)=\Lambda_{-j}^{-1}-U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}.

The following lemma provides the estimates of
|\alpha_{j}(z)|,\|b_{j}(z)\|,
and the smallest singular value
s_{\min}(D_{j}(z))
on the circle
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. Its proof is deferred to Section
9.1.2
.

L
EMMA
9.2
.

Fix
j\in[r_{+}]
. Assume
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}+100\rho
and
\lambda_{j}
is a simple eigenvalue of
A
. Assume the following gap condition holds:

\displaystyle\min_{l<j}(\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j})

\displaystyle\geq 50\rho\quad\text{and}\quad\min_{l\in\mathcal{O}_{+},\,l>j}(\vartheta_{j}-\vartheta_{l})\geq 50\rho.

(9.6)

where
\mathcal{O}_{+}:=\{l\in[r_{+}]:\lambda_{l}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}\}
is the index set for the supercritical eigenvalues.
Then for
z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{4\vartheta_{j}^{2}}\leq|\alpha_{j}(z)|\leq\frac{3\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}},

(9.7)

\displaystyle\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{1}{10}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}},

(9.8)

\displaystyle s_{\min}(D_{j}(z))\geq 3\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.9)

In particular, (
9.9
) suggests that
D_{j}(z)
is invertible on
\Gamma_{j}(z)
and

\displaystyle\|D^{-1}_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{1}{3}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}.

(9.10)

Define

h_{j}(z):=\alpha_{j}(z)-b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)

for
z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. By Lemma
9.2
,
h_{j}(z)
is well-defined on
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. Besides,

|h_{j}(z)|\geq|\alpha_{j}(z)|-\|b_{j}(z)\|^{2}\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|\geq\frac{\rho}{4\vartheta_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{300}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}=\frac{37}{150}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}>0

and

\displaystyle|h_{j}^{-1}(z)|\leq\frac{150}{37}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}.

(9.11)

Now we are ready to estimate
\|K^{-1}(z)\|
on
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. By Schur’s complement,

\displaystyle K(z)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}h_{j}^{-1}(z)&h_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\\
h_{j}^{-1}(z)D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)&D_{j}^{-1}(z)+h_{j}^{-1}(z)D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\end{pmatrix}

We decompose
K(z)^{-1}
as

\displaystyle K(z)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&D_{j}^{-1}(z)\end{pmatrix}+h_{j}^{-1}(z)\begin{pmatrix}1&b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\\
D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)&D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\end{pmatrix}.

Note that by setting
\mathtt{v}:=b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)
, we have

\mathtt{H}:=\begin{pmatrix}I&b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\\
D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)&D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\mathtt{v}^{\mathrm{T}}\\
\mathtt{v}&\mathtt{v}\mathtt{v}^{\mathrm{T}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
\mathtt{v}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\mathtt{v}^{\mathrm{T}}\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\|\mathtt{H}\|=1+\|\mathtt{v}\|^{2}=1+\|b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|^{2}\leq 1+\|b_{j}(z)\|^{2}\cdot\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|K(z)^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|+|h_{j}^{-1}(z)|\cdot\|\mathtt{H}\|

(9.12)

\displaystyle\leq\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|+|h_{j}^{-1}(z)|\left(1+\|b_{j}(z)\|^{2}\cdot\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|^{2}\right).

(9.13)

Plugging the bounds in Lemma
9.2
, (
9.10
), and (
9.11
), we get

\displaystyle\|K(z)^{-1}\|\leq\frac{1}{3}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}+\frac{150}{37}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}\left(1+\frac{1}{900}\right)<\frac{9}{2}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}.

(9.14)

Together with (
9.5
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|K^{-1}(z)B(z)\|\leq\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|K^{-1}(z)\|\cdot\sup_{z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)}\|B(z)\|\leq\frac{9}{2}\frac{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}{\rho}\cdot\left(\frac{100}{99}\right)^{2}\frac{M}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}<1,

where we used
\rho\geq 5M
in the last inequality.

By the assumption (
9.6
),
K(z)
has exactly one root
\vartheta_{j}
inside
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. By Lemma
9.1
, there is exactly one eigenvalue
\widehat{\lambda}
of
\widetilde{A}
lying in the cycle
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. To complete the proof, it remains to show that
\widehat{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
.

To establish the index matching
\widehat{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
, it suffices to verify the following eigenvalue counts for the perturbed matrix
\widetilde{A}
:

1.

There are exactly
j-1
eigenvalues lie strictly to the right of
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
;

2.

There are exactly
n-j
eigenvalues lie strictly to the left of
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
.

As noted previously, the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{A}
outside
[-\|E\|,\|E\|]
are exactly the zeros of
f(z)=\det(K(z)-B(z))
, with matching algebraic multiplicities. To establish the above eigenvalue counts, we define new contours as follows. Recall that
\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{l},\rho):=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-\vartheta_{l}|\leq\rho\}
denotes the disk centered as
\vartheta_{l}
with radius
\rho
. Set

\displaystyle D_{j}^{+}:=\bigcup_{l<j}\,{\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{l},\rho)},\qquad D_{j}^{-}:=D_{j}^{+}\cup{\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho)},

(9.15)

and let
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}:=\partial D_{j}^{\pm}
be positively oriented on each connected component. By our gap assumption (
9.6
), the disk
{\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho)}
is disjoint from
D_{j}^{+}
, and every supercritical root excluded from
D_{j}^{\pm}
lies with distance at least
49\rho
from
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
.

Define the auxiliary matrix

\displaystyle A_{0}(z):=\mathrm{diag}(\alpha_{1}(z),\dots,\alpha_{r}(z))\quad\text{with}\quad\alpha_{l}(z):=\lambda_{l}^{-1}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}.

(9.16)

We show the next result whose proof is deferred to Section
9.1.1

L
EMMA
9.3
.

Under the assumption of Lemma
9.2
, we have

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}(K(z)-A_{0}(z))\|<1,\qquad\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|K(z)^{-1}B(z)\|<1.

Hence, by Lemma
9.1
,
\det(A_{0}(z))
,
g(z)=\det(K(z))
, and
f(z)=\det(K(z)-B(z))
have the same number of zeros inside each region
D_{j}^{\pm}
.
Now

\det(A_{0}(z))=\prod_{l=1}^{r}\alpha_{l}(z),

and among these factors on the right-hand side, only
\alpha_{l}(z)
with
l\in\mathcal{O}_{+}
have zeros in the supercritical regime, namely at
z=\vartheta_{l}
(see Proposition
5.1
). By construction,
D_{j}^{+}
contains exactly the roots
\vartheta_{1},\dots,\vartheta_{j-1}
, while
D_{j}^{-}
contains exactly the roots
\vartheta_{1},\dots,\vartheta_{j}
. Therefore,
f(z)
has exactly
j-1
zeros in
D_{j}^{+}
and exactly
j
zeros in
D_{j}^{-}
.

Since
D_{j}^{-}\setminus D_{j}^{+}=\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho)
and
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)=\partial\,\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho)
, it follows that
\det(K-B)
has exactly one zero in
\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{j},\rho)
. This is precisely the eigenvalue
\widehat{\lambda}
enclosed by
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. Therefore there are exactly
j-1
eigenvalues of
\widetilde{A}
strictly to the right of
\widehat{\lambda}
, and hence

\widehat{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}.

9.1.1
Proof of Lemma
9.3

We estimate
\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}\|
,
\|K(z)-A_{0}(z)\|
, and
\|K^{-1}(z)\|
respectively for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
. Since

K(z)-A_{0}(z)=-\mathrm{Off}(U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U),

we have

\|K(z)-A_{0}(z)\|\leq\|\mathrm{Off}(U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U)\|.

Claim.
For
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}},

(9.17)

\displaystyle\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{2}{25}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}},

(9.18)

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Off}(U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U)\|\leq\frac{1}{5}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}},

(9.19)

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Off}(U^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U)\|\leq\frac{1}{10}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

(9.20)

We defer the proof of this claim to the end of this section. Using these bounds, we first have

\displaystyle\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}\|=\max_{l\in[r]}|\alpha_{l}^{-1}(z)=\frac{1}{|\min_{l\in[r]}\alpha_{l}(z)|}\leq 2\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}

Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}(K(z)-A_{0}(z))\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}\|\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|K(z)-A_{0}(z))\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}\cdot\frac{1}{5}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}<1.

Next, we estimate
\|K(z)^{-1}\|
for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
, which is similar to the previous estimates on
\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
. Since
\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\Big\|\leq\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U\big)\Big\|
, from (
9.30
) and by the
Claim
, we get

\displaystyle s_{\min}(D_{j}(z))

\displaystyle\geq\min_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\alpha_{l}(z)|-\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\Big\|

(9.21)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}-\frac{1}{5}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}=\frac{3}{10}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

(9.22)

and thus

\displaystyle\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|\leq\frac{10}{3}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}.

Recall that
h_{j}(z)=\alpha_{j}(z)-b_{j}(z)^{\mathrm{T}}D_{j}^{-1}(z)b_{j}(z)
. By the claim,

|h_{j}(z)|\geq|\alpha_{j}(z)|-\|b_{j}(z)\|^{2}\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}-\left(\frac{2}{25}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{3}{10}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}=\frac{359}{750}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

Hence,
|h_{j}^{-1}(z)|\leq\frac{750}{359}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}
and by (
9.12
),

\displaystyle\|K(z)^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|+|h_{j}^{-1}(z)|\left(1+\|b_{j}(z)\|^{2}\cdot\|D_{j}^{-1}(z)\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10}{3}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}+\frac{750}{359}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}\left(1+\left(\frac{2}{25}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{3}{10}\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle<5.5\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}.

Finally,

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|K^{-1}(z)B(z)\|\leq\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|K^{-1}(z)\|\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}}\|B(z)\|\leq 5.5\frac{|z|^{2}}{\rho}\frac{M}{|z|^{2}}<1,

where we used
\rho\geq 10M
in the last inequality.

To finish the proof, it remains to prove the claim.

Proof of (
9.17
)

We bound
|\alpha_{l}(z)|
for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
. The proof is similar to that of (
9.39
). We split the discussion into three cases:
l\in\mathcal{O}_{+},

l\in[r_{+}]\setminus\mathcal{O}_{+}
, and
l\in[r]\setminus[r_{+}]
.

Case 1:

l\in\mathcal{O}_{+}
. We have
\lambda_{l}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. By Proposition
5.1
, define
\vartheta_{l}
as the unique solution to
\alpha_{l}(z)=0
. Also,
\vartheta_{l}\geq 5\sqrt{R_{\max}}
.

For
z=x+\sqrt{-1}y\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
, we have
|y|\leq\rho
and
x\geq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}>4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. Note that using the same estimates as (
9.28
) and (
9.29
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{4}{5}\frac{1}{x^{2}}\leq\alpha^{\prime}_{l}(x)=-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime}(x)u_{l}\leq\frac{3}{x^{2}}.

(9.23)

Indeed, we refined the constant for the lower bound using
x\geq 4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)=-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime}(x)u_{l}\geq\frac{1}{x^{2}}-\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{x^{6}}\geq\frac{1}{x^{2}}\left(1-\frac{243}{5\cdot 256}\right)>\frac{4}{5x^{2}}.

Since
\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{l})=0
, from
|\alpha_{l}(x)|=|\int_{\vartheta_{l}}^{x}\alpha^{\prime}_{l}(t)\,dt|
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{4}{5}\frac{|x-\vartheta_{l}|}{x\vartheta_{l}}\leq|\alpha_{l}(x)|\leq 3\frac{|x-\vartheta_{l}|}{x\vartheta_{l}}.

(9.24)

Now the Taylor expansion of
\alpha_{l}(z)
at
x
yields:

\alpha_{l}(z)=\alpha_{l}(x)+\sqrt{-1}y\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{l}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)(\sqrt{-1}y)^{2},

where
\zeta
lies in a vertical segment connecting
x
and
z
. In particular,
|\zeta|\geq x\geq 4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. It follows from part (i) of Lemma
5.1
that

|\alpha_{l}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)|=|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)u_{l}|\leq\|\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)\|\leq\frac{28}{|\zeta|^{3}}\leq\frac{28}{x^{3}}.

Next, plugging in (
9.23
) and (
9.24
) yields

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(x)+\sqrt{-1}y\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{l}(x)^{2}+y^{2}\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)^{2}}

(9.25)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{16}{25}\frac{(x-\vartheta_{l})^{2}}{x^{2}\vartheta_{l}^{2}}+\frac{16}{25}\frac{y^{2}}{x^{4}}}=\frac{4}{5}\frac{1}{x^{2}}\sqrt{(x-\vartheta_{l})^{2}\frac{x^{2}}{\vartheta_{l}^{2}}+y^{2}}.

(9.26)

If
\vartheta_{l}\leq\frac{100}{99}x
, then
x/\vartheta_{l}\geq 0.99
and

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(x)+\sqrt{-1}y\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)|\geq\frac{4}{5x^{2}}0.99\sqrt{(x-\vartheta_{l})^{2}+y^{2}}=0.792\frac{|z-\vartheta_{l}|}{x^{2}}\geq 0.792\frac{\rho}{x^{2}}.

If
\vartheta_{l}>\frac{100}{99}x
, we use the fact that the function
f(t)=\frac{t-x}{xt}
is increasing for
t>x
. Its minimum value occurs at the boundary
t=\frac{100}{99}x
. By (
9.24
),

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(x)+\sqrt{-1}y\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)|\geq|\alpha_{l}(x)|\geq\frac{4}{5}\frac{\vartheta_{l}-x}{x\vartheta_{l}}\geq\frac{4}{5}\frac{\frac{100}{99}x-x}{x\cdot\frac{100}{99}x}=\frac{4x}{500x^{2}}.

Since
x\geq 99\rho
, we have
\frac{4x}{500x^{2}}\geq\frac{4\cdot 99\rho}{500x^{2}}=0.792\frac{\rho}{x^{2}}
.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq|\alpha_{l}(x)+\sqrt{-1}y\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(x)|-14\frac{y^{2}}{x^{3}}

\displaystyle\geq 0.792\frac{\rho}{x^{2}}-\frac{14}{99}\frac{\rho}{x^{2}}>\frac{\rho}{2x^{2}}\geq\frac{\rho}{2|z|^{2}},

where we used
|z|\geq x\geq 99\rho\geq 99|y|
.

Case 2:

l\in[r_{+}]\setminus\mathcal{O}_{+}
. The lower bound on
|\alpha_{l}(z)|
follows the same approach as (
9.37
). We skip the proof. In this case, we have

|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq 70\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}\geq 70\left(\frac{99}{100}\right)^{2}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}>60\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

Case 3:

l\in[r]\setminus[r_{+}]
. We have
\lambda_{l}<0
. For
z=x+\sqrt{-1}y\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\pm}
, we have
x\geq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. From (
5.4
), we expand

\displaystyle\alpha_{l}(z)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}}-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{1}{z^{3}}u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{l}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{l}.

Note that

\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}}-\frac{1}{z}\right|\geq\left|\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{l}}-\frac{1}{z}\right)\right|=\frac{1}{|\lambda_{l}|}+\frac{x}{|z|^{2}}>\frac{x}{|z|^{2}}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{z^{3}}u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{l}+u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{l}\right|\leq\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}+\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}\leq\frac{173}{128}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}\leq\frac{173}{128\cdot 16}\frac{x}{|z|^{2}}

using the bound
|z|\geq x\geq 4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq\frac{x}{|z|^{2}}-\frac{173}{128\cdot 16}\frac{x}{|z|^{2}}>90\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we have proved (
9.17
).

Proof of (
9.18
)

The proof is almost identical to that of (
9.8
). We briefly it here. Following the same approach as (
9.8
), we first get

\displaystyle\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{2|z|^{3}}+\frac{15R_{\max}}{2|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})<\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}.

Since
|z|\geq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}
and

\rho\geq 13\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}},

we further get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\leq\frac{100}{99}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}\leq\frac{100}{99\cdot 13}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}},

(9.27)

and hence

\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{2}{25}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}.

Proofs of (
9.19
) and (
9.20
)

The bound for
\mathrm{Off}\big(U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U\big)
are almost identical to the bound for
\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)
derived in (
9.9
). We briefly sketch it here.
It follows from the same steps that

\|\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U_{-j})\|\leq\frac{15R_{\max}}{\sqrt{2}|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})\leq\frac{15}{9\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{10}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{2|z|^{3}}+\frac{15R_{\max}}{\sqrt{2}|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{15}{9\sqrt{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{5}\frac{\rho}{|z|^{2}}

where we used (
9.27
).

9.1.2
Proof of Lemma
9.2

Recall from (
9.4
) that

\rho=5M+15\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\lambda_{j}}.

By our assumption,
\rho\leq\lambda_{j}/100
. From the estimates in (
9.2
), we also have

\rho\geq 15\cdot 0.87\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}}>13\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}}.

For
z\in\Gamma_{j}(\rho)
satisfying
|z-\vartheta_{j}|=\rho
, we have

\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}\leq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\leq|z|\leq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\leq\frac{101}{100}\vartheta_{j}.

Proof of (
9.7
).

We first estimate
|\alpha_{j}(z)|
. Recall that
\alpha_{j}(\vartheta_{j})=0
. By Taylor’s theorem,

\alpha_{j}(z)=\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\vartheta_{j})(z-\vartheta_{j})+\mathtt{E}_{2},

where

|\mathtt{E}_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2}|z-\vartheta_{j}|^{2}\max_{|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho}|\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)|=\frac{1}{2}\rho^{2}\max_{|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho}|\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)|.

By part (ii) of Lemma
5.1
(see also (
A.19
)),

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\vartheta_{j})=-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime}(\vartheta_{j})u_{j}\geq\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}-\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{\vartheta_{j}^{6}}\geq\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}\left(1-\frac{243}{5\cdot 81}\right)=\frac{2}{5\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.28)

For
\zeta
satisfying
|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho
, we have
|\zeta|\geq\vartheta_{j}-\rho\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}>4\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. It follows from part (i) of Lemma
5.1
that

|\alpha_{j}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)|=|u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)u_{j}|\leq\|\Phi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)\|\leq\frac{28}{|\zeta|^{3}}\leq 28\left(\frac{100}{99}\right)^{3}\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}<\frac{29}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}.

Thus,

|\mathtt{E}_{2}|\leq\frac{29}{2}\frac{\rho^{2}}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}\leq\frac{29}{200}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}

and

|\alpha_{j}(z)|\geq\frac{2\rho}{5\vartheta_{j}^{2}}-\frac{29}{200}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}>\frac{\rho}{4\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

For the upper bound, observe that

|\alpha_{j}(z)|=|\alpha_{j}(z)-\alpha_{j}(\vartheta_{j})|\leq\max_{|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho}|\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\zeta)|\cdot\rho.

By part (ii) of Lemma
5.1
(see also (
A.19
)), we have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{1}{|\zeta|^{2}}+\frac{3\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{|\zeta|^{4}}+\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|\zeta|^{6}}\leq\frac{2}{|\zeta|^{2}}\leq\frac{3}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}

(9.29)

using
|\zeta|\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}
and
\vartheta_{j}>5\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. Hence,

|\alpha_{j}(z)|\leq\frac{3\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

Proof of (
9.8
).

Next, we estimate
\|b_{j}(z)\|=\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}\|
. Since
U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}u_{j}=0
, using the expansion of
\Phi(z)
in (
5.4
), we immediately get

\displaystyle b_{j}(z)=\frac{1}{z^{3}}U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{j}+U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{j}\|}{|z|^{3}}+\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j}\|.

Since
U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}u_{j}=0
,

\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{j}\|=\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{R}-cI)u_{j}\|\leq\|\mathcal{R}-cI\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})

by selecting
c=\frac{1}{2}(R_{\max}+R_{\min})
. Similarly, we also have

\|U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j}\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(z))\leq\frac{15R_{\max}}{2|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}),

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
5.2
. Thus

\displaystyle\|b_{j}(z)\|\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{2|z|^{3}}+\frac{15R_{\max}}{2|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})<\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\leq\left(\frac{100}{99}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}<\frac{1}{10}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}

by our assumption
\rho\geq 15\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\lambda_{j}}
.

Proof of (
9.9
).

Finally, we show the lower bound on
s_{\min}(D_{j}(z)).
For each
l\in[r]
, set

\alpha_{l}(z):=\lambda_{l}^{-1}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}.

We rewrite

\displaystyle D_{j}(z)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{-j}^{-1}-U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{-j}^{-1}-\mathrm{diag}(u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l})_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}-\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(\alpha_{l}(z))_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}-\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big).

Thus,

\displaystyle s_{\min}(D_{j}(z))\geq\min_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\alpha_{l}(z)|-\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\Big\|.

(9.30)

We first bound
\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)
. By (
5.4
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Off}\big(\frac{1}{z}I+\frac{1}{z^{3}}U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}U_{-j}+U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U_{-j}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Off}\big(\frac{1}{z^{3}}U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}U_{-j}\big)+\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U_{-j}\big).

(9.31)

Note that

\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{R}-c_{1}I)U_{-j})=\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}U_{-j}).

Choosing
c_{1}=\frac{1}{2}(R_{\max}+R_{\min})
, we get

\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}U_{-j}\big)\Big\|\leq\|\mathcal{R}-c_{1}I\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Similarly, we also have

\|\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U_{-j})\|=\|\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}(\varepsilon(z)-c_{2}I)U_{-j})\|\leq\|\varepsilon(z)-c_{2}I\|.

Decompose
\varepsilon(z)=\mathrm{diag}(\varepsilon_{k}(z))=\mathrm{diag}(x_{k})+\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{diag}(y_{k})
. Set
c_{2}=c_{x}+\sqrt{-1}c_{y}=\frac{1}{2}(\max_{k}x_{k}+\min_{k}x_{k})+\sqrt{-1}(\max_{k}y_{k}+\min_{k}y_{k}).
Then

|\varepsilon_{k}(z)-c_{2}|^{2}=|x_{k}-c_{x}|^{2}+|y_{k}-c_{y}|^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{osc}(x)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{osc}(y)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(z))^{2}.

It follows that

\|\mathrm{Off}(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)U_{-j})\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(z))\leq\frac{15R_{\max}}{\sqrt{2}|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

The last inequality is by Lemma
5.2
. Combining these estimates with (
9.1.2
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{2|z|^{3}}+\frac{15R_{\max}}{\sqrt{2}|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})

(9.32)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{15}{9\sqrt{2}}\right)

(9.33)

\displaystyle<1.8\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}\leq\frac{1.8}{13}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}

(9.34)

where we used
|z|\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}
and
\rho>13\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}}
.

It remains to bound
\min_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\alpha_{l}(z)|
where

\alpha_{l}(z)=\lambda_{l}^{-1}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}.

Define

\mathcal{O}_{+}:=\{l\in[r_{+}]:\lambda_{l}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}\}.

We split the discussion into three cases:
l\in\mathcal{O}_{+}
,
l\in[r_{+}]\setminus\mathcal{O}_{+}
and
l\in[r]\setminus[r_{+}]
.

Case 1:

l\in\mathcal{O}_{+}
. We have
\lambda_{l}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. By Proposition
5.1
, define
\vartheta_{l}
as the unique solution to
\alpha_{l}(z)=0
. Also,
\vartheta_{l}\geq 5\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. Using similar estimates as (
9.28
), we get, for any real
t\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
,

\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(t)=-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime}(t)u_{l}\geq\frac{2}{5t^{2}}.

Since
\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{l})=0
,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|=\left|\int_{\vartheta_{l}}^{\vartheta_{j}}\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(t)\,dt\right|\geq\left|\int_{\vartheta_{l}}^{\vartheta_{j}}\frac{2}{5t^{2}}\,dt\right|=\frac{2}{5}\frac{|\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j}|}{\vartheta_{l}\vartheta_{j}}\geq\frac{2}{5}\frac{|\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j}|}{\vartheta_{j}(\vartheta_{j}+|\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j}|)}.

The last step is from
\vartheta_{l}\leq\vartheta_{j}+|\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j}|.

Since
|\vartheta_{l}-\vartheta_{j}|\geq 50\rho
by our assumption (
9.6
), together with
\rho\leq\vartheta_{j}/100
, we get

|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|\geq\frac{40}{3}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

Next, using the same estimates as in (
9.29
), we also have

|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})-\alpha_{l}(z)|\leq\max_{|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho}|\alpha_{l}^{\prime}(\zeta)|\cdot\rho\leq 3\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|-|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})-\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq\left(\frac{40}{3}-3\right)\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}=\frac{10}{3}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.35)

Case 2:

l\in[r_{+}]\setminus\mathcal{O}_{+}
. We have
0<\lambda_{l}<6\sqrt{R_{\max}}\leq\lambda_{j}
. From

\alpha_{l}(z)-\alpha_{j}(z)=\left(\lambda_{l}^{-1}-\lambda_{j}^{-1}\right)-\left(u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}\right),

observe that

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)-\alpha_{j}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{j}\lambda_{l}}-\left|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}\right|.

(9.36)

Furthermore, by (
5.4
),

\displaystyle\left|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{z^{3}}u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{l}-\frac{1}{z^{3}}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{j}-(u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|z|^{3}}|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}u_{j}|+|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}+\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(z)).

For the last inequality above, since
u_{l},u_{j}
are unit vectors, their quadratic forms represent convex combinations of the diagonal entries. Therefore, the difference between them is strictly bounded by the maximum pairwise distance in the complex plane, which is the oscillation.

By Lemma
5.2
,

\left|u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}\right|\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}\left(1+\frac{15R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{8}{3}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}.

Continuing from (
9.36
), together with
|\alpha_{j}(z)|\leq\frac{3\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}
from (
9.7
) and the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq|\alpha_{l}(z)-\alpha_{j}(z)|-|\alpha_{j}(z)|\geq\frac{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{l}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}-\frac{8}{3}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{|z|^{3}}-\frac{3\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

Since
\lambda_{l}<6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
and
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}+100\rho
, we have
\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{l}\geq 100\rho
. Using our assumptions,
|z|\geq\frac{99}{100}\vartheta_{j}
,
0.87\lambda_{j}\leq\vartheta_{j}\leq 1.16\lambda_{j}
from (
9.2
), and
\rho>13\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}}
. Thus we get

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq(100\cdot 0.87^{2}-3)\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}-\frac{8}{3}\left(\frac{100}{99}\right)^{3}\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}>70\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.37)

Case 3:

l\in[r]\setminus[r_{+}]
. We have
\lambda_{l}<0
. Using the expansion (
5.4
), we get

\displaystyle u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\vartheta_{j})u_{l}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{ll}}{\vartheta_{j}^{3}}+\epsilon_{l}(\vartheta_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}-\|\epsilon_{l}(\vartheta_{j})\|\geq\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}-\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{\vartheta_{j}^{5}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}-\frac{5}{72}\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}=\frac{67}{72}\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}.

Thus,
\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})=\lambda_{l}^{-1}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\vartheta_{j})u_{l}<0
and

|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|\geq\frac{67}{72}\frac{1}{\vartheta_{j}}\geq\frac{6700}{72}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

Using similar estimates as (
9.28
), we have
|\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{3}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}
for any
\zeta
satisfying
|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho
. Consequently,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)-\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|\leq\rho\cdot\max_{|\zeta-\vartheta_{j}|\leq\rho}|\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(\zeta)|\leq 3\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}

and

\displaystyle|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq|\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|-|\alpha_{l}(z)-\alpha_{l}(\vartheta_{j})|\geq\left(\frac{6700}{72}-3\right)\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}>30\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.38)

Finally, combining (
9.35
), (
9.37
), and (
9.38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\min_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\alpha_{l}(z)|\geq\frac{10}{3}\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

(9.39)

Continuing from (
9.30
), together with (
9.32
), we arrive at

\displaystyle s_{\min}(D_{j}(z))\geq\min_{l\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\alpha_{l}(z)|-\Big\|\mathrm{Off}\big(U_{-j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U_{-j}\big)\Big\|\geq\left(\frac{10}{3}-\frac{1.8}{13}\right)\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}>3\frac{\rho}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}.

This completes the proof.

9.2
Proof of Theorem
6

Theorem
6
will be proved using similar techniques and estimates as Theorem
5
. We briefly sketch it here. Recall that
\rho\equiv\rho_{j}=10M+15\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})}{\lambda_{j}}
.

To establish the lower bound for the top-
j
outlier cluster, we define the block contour enclosing the deterministic roots
\vartheta_{1},\dots,\vartheta_{j}
. Following our previous notation (
9.15
), let

\displaystyle D_{j}^{-}:=\bigcup_{l=1}^{j}\mathtt{D}(\vartheta_{l},\rho_{j}),

and let
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{-}:=\partial D_{j}^{-}
be its positively oriented boundary. By our gap assumption
\min_{l>k}(\underline{\vartheta}_{k}-\vartheta_{l})\geq 50\rho_{k}
, every supercritical root excluded from
D_{k}^{-}
lies at a distance
49\rho
from
\mathcal{C}_{k}^{-}
, ensuring the contour is strictly isolated.

Recall from (
9.16
) that

A_{0}(z):=\mathrm{diag}(\alpha_{1}(z),\dots,\alpha_{r}(z))\quad\text{with}\quad\alpha_{l}(z):=\lambda_{l}^{-1}-u_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{l}.

The main goal is to establish an analogous result to Lemma
9.3
:

L
EMMA
9.4
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
6
, we have

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{-}}\|A_{0}(z)^{-1}(K(z)-A_{0}(z))\|<1,\qquad\sup_{z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{-}}\|K(z)^{-1}B(z)\|<1.

Because the boundary
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{-}
maintains a distance of at least
\rho_{j}
from any enclosed root
\vartheta_{l}
, the uniform estimates established in the
Claim
of Section
9.1.1
hold identically for all
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{-}
. Consequently, Lemma
9.4
holds.

Hence, by Lemma
9.4
and Lemma
9.1
,
\det(A_{0}(z))
,
g(z)=\det(K(z))
, and
f(z)=\det(K(z)-B(z))
have the same number of zeros inside the region
D_{j}^{-}
.

Since
\det(A_{0}(z))=\prod_{l=1}^{r}\alpha_{l}(z)
, its zeros in the supercritical regime occur at
\vartheta_{l}
’s. By construction,
D_{j}^{-}
contains exactly
j
such roots:
\vartheta_{1},\dots,\vartheta_{j}
. Therefore,
f(z)
has exactly
j
zeros inside
D_{j}^{-}
. As noted previously, the zeros of
f(z)
outside
[-\|E\|,\|E\|]
correspond exactly to the eigenvalues of the perturbed matrix
\widetilde{A}
. Since the eigenvalues are strictly ordered (i.e.,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}\geq\dots\geq\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}
), these
j
enclosed roots inside
D_{j}^{-}
must be exactly the top
j
perturbed eigenvalues
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
. Because all
j
of these eigenvalues lie strictly inside
D_{j}^{-}
, their locations are bounded from below by the leftmost boundary of the region.

Recalling that
\underline{\vartheta}_{j}:=\min_{1\leq l\leq j}\vartheta_{l}
, the real part of any
z\in D_{k}^{-}
satisfies
\operatorname{Re}(z)\geq\underline{\vartheta}_{k}-\rho_{k}
. Thus, we conclude that for all
s\in[k]
,

\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\geq\underline{\vartheta}_{k}-\rho_{k}.

The proof is complete.

Appendix A
Proofs of Lemmas
5.1
, Lemma
5.2
, Proposition
5.1
, and Lemma
5.4

A.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Fix
z
with
|z|^{2}\geq 6R_{\max}
.

Proof of Part (i)
:
Define a mapping
F:\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}
componentwise as

\left(F(y)\right)_{i}=\frac{1}{z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}y_{j}}=\frac{1}{z-(\Sigma y)_{i}}.

Consider the metric
\|y\|_{\infty}=\max_{i}|y_{i}|
on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Note that
\phi
is a fixed point of
F
, i.e.,
F(\phi)=\phi
. Define a closed set

\mathcal{B}:=\left\{y\in\mathbb{C}^{n}:\|y\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}\right\}.

We first show
F(\mathcal{B})\subseteq\mathcal{B}
. For
y\in\mathcal{B}
and every
i\in[n]
,

|(\Sigma y)_{i}|=\big|\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}y_{j}\big|\leq\|y\|_{\infty}R_{\max}\leq\frac{2}{|z|}R_{\max}\leq\frac{|z|}{3}

by our supposition
|z|^{2}\geq 6R_{\max}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\big|z-(\Sigma y)_{i}\big|\geq|z|-\big|(\Sigma y)_{i}\big|\geq\frac{2}{3}|z|

(A.1)

and

\big|\left(F(y)\right)_{i}\big|=\frac{1}{|z-(\Sigma y)_{i}|}\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}.

This shows that
F(y)\in\mathcal{B}.

Next, we show that
F:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{B}
is a contraction. Take
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
. For each
i\in[n]
, using (
A.1
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\left(F(y)\right)_{i}-\left(F(y^{\prime})\right)_{i}\big|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(y_{j}-y_{j}^{\prime})}{(z-(\Sigma y)_{i})(z-(\Sigma y^{\prime})_{i})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{\max}\|y-y^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{(2|z|/3)^{2}}=\frac{9}{4}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\|y-y^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3}{8}\|y-y^{\prime}\|_{\infty}.

Since
(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
is a complete metric space, by Banach’s fixed point theorem,
F
has a unique fixed point in
y^{*}\in\mathcal{B}
. Since the solution
\phi
to the QVE (
5.1
) is unique and
\phi(z)\to 0
as
|z|\to\infty
. Therefore,
\phi=y^{*}\in\mathcal{B}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\max_{i}|\phi_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}.

(A.2)

We have

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\Sigma\phi(z)\big)_{i}\Big|=\Big|\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)\Big|\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}R_{\max}.

(A.3)

Hence,
\Big|\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)\Big|\leq\frac{1}{4}|z|
and

\frac{3}{4}|z|\leq\big|z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)\big|\leq\frac{5}{4}|z|.

To show the bounds on
\phi^{\prime}(z)
, differentiating the equation

\phi_{i}(z)^{-1}=z-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)

gives

-\phi_{i}(z)^{-2}\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)=1-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z),

hence

\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)=-\phi_{i}(z)^{2}\Bigl(1-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)\Bigr)

and

|\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq|\phi_{i}(z)|^{2}\Bigl(1+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)|\Bigr).

Let
M_{1}:=\max_{i}|\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)|
. By (
A.2
),

M_{1}\leq\frac{9}{4|z|^{2}}(1+R_{\max}M_{1}).

Since
R_{\max}/|z|^{2}\leq 1/6
, we solve
M_{1}\leq\frac{9}{4|z|^{2}}+\frac{3}{8}M_{1}
to obtain

M_{1}=\max_{i}|\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq\frac{18}{5|z|^{2}}.

To bound
\phi^{\prime\prime}(z)
, similarly, differentiating the equation one more time yields

\phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}(z)=-2\phi_{i}(z)\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)\Bigl(1-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)\Bigr)+\phi_{i}(z)^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}^{\prime\prime}(z).

Hence,

|\phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}(z)|\leq 2|\phi_{i}(z)|\,|\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\Bigl(1+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)|\Bigr)+|\phi_{i}(z)|^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\phi_{j}^{\prime\prime}(z)|.

Let
M_{2}:=\max_{i}|\phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}(z)|
. Using the previous bounds on
M_{1}
and (
A.2
), we have

M_{2}\leq 2\cdot\frac{3}{2|z|}\cdot\frac{18}{5|z|^{2}}\cdot\Bigl(1+\frac{3}{5}\Bigr)+\frac{9R_{\max}}{4|z|^{2}}M_{2}.

Thus,

M_{2}=\max_{i}|\phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}(z)|\leq\frac{3456}{125|z|^{3}}\leq\frac{28}{|z|^{3}}.

Proof of Part (ii)
:
In the following, we prove (
5.3
). From
\phi_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z-(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}
and the exact identity
\frac{1}{z-x}=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{x}{z(z-x)}
, treating
x=(\Sigma\phi)_{i}
, we get

\displaystyle\phi_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z(z-(\Sigma\phi)_{i})}=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z}\phi_{i}(z):=\frac{1}{z}+\delta_{i}(z),

(A.4)

where

|\delta_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{|(\Sigma\phi)_{i}|}{|z|}|\phi_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{9}{4}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}

by (
A.2
) and (
A.3
). Next, from the algebraic identity
\frac{1}{z-x}=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{x}{z^{2}}+\frac{x^{2}}{z^{2}(z-x)}
, we also have

\displaystyle\phi_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z^{2}}+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}^{2}}{z^{2}}\phi_{i}(z):=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z^{2}}+\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z),

(A.5)

where, by (
A.2
) and (
A.3
), we have the estimate

|\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{|(\Sigma\phi)_{i}|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\big|\phi_{i}(z)\big|\leq\frac{27}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}.

Now we plug (
A.4
) into the term
\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z^{2}}
in (
A.5
):

\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z^{2}}=\frac{1}{z^{2}}\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{z}+\delta_{j}(z)\right)=\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+\frac{\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z)}{z^{2}}.

Observe that

\left|\frac{\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z)}{z^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\max_{j}|\delta_{j}(z)|\leq\frac{9}{4}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}.

Combining the above estimates, we arrive at the final expansion:

\phi_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+\left(\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z)+\frac{\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z)}{z^{2}}\right):=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+\varepsilon_{i}(z)

with the error term
\varepsilon_{i}(z)
satisfying

|\varepsilon_{i}(z)|\leq|\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z)|+\left|\frac{\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z)}{z^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{27}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}+\frac{9}{4}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}=\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{5}}.

This proves (
5.3
).

To prove (
5.5
), we start with

\phi_{i}(z)=\left(\frac{1}{z}+\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+\varepsilon_{i}(z)\right)^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{z^{2}}-\frac{3R_{i}}{z^{4}}+\varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}(z).

Recall that

\varepsilon_{i}(z)=\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z)+\frac{1}{z^{2}}\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z),

where

\delta_{i}(z)=\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z}\phi_{i}(z)\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{\delta}_{i}(z)=\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}^{2}}{z^{2}}\phi_{i}(z).

It remains to bound
|\varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}(z)|
. Note that

\displaystyle\delta_{i}^{\prime}(z)=-\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z^{2}}\phi_{i}(z)+\frac{(\Sigma\phi^{\prime})_{i}}{z}\phi_{i}(z)+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}}{z}\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z),

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}(z)=-2\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}^{2}}{z^{3}}\phi_{i}(z)+2\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}(\Sigma\phi^{\prime})_{i}}{z^{2}}\phi_{i}(z)+\frac{(\Sigma\phi)_{i}^{2}}{z^{2}}\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z).

Plugging in the previous estimates

|\phi_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{3}{2|z|},\quad|\phi_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq\frac{18}{5|z|^{2}},\quad|(\Sigma\phi)_{i}|\leq\frac{3R_{\max}}{2|z|},\quad|(\Sigma\phi^{\prime})_{i}|\leq\frac{18R_{\max}}{5|z|^{2}}

yields

|\delta_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq 13.05\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{4}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\tilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq 31.05\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{6}}.

Together with
|\delta_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{9}{4}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}(z)|\leq|\tilde{\delta}_{i}^{\prime}(z)|+\left|-\frac{2}{z^{3}}\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}(z)+\frac{1}{z^{2}}\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\delta_{j}^{\prime}(z)\right|\leq 48.6\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{6}}.

We have proved (
5.5
).

A.2
Proof of Lemma
5.2

Fix
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z|\geq\sqrt{6R_{\max}}>0
. Let
\sqrt{\cdot}
denote the principal branch of the complex square root on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0]
. Recall that in (
5.1
),
\phi_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)}
. For comparison, for each
i\in[k]
, if
R_{i}>0
, define an auxiliary function

\displaystyle m_{i}(z)=\frac{z-\sqrt{z^{2}-4R_{i}}}{2R_{i}}=\frac{z}{2R_{i}}\left(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{4R_{i}}{z^{2}}}\right)

(A.6)

and if
R_{i}=0
, define
m_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z}
. Then
m_{i}(z)
satisfies the quadratic equation

\displaystyle m_{i}(z)=\frac{1}{z-R_{i}m_{i}(z)}.

(A.7)

Set

d_{i}(z)=\phi_{i}(z)-m_{i}(z)\quad\text{and}\quad d(z)=(d_{1}(z),\dots,d_{n}(z)).

Then

\displaystyle\Phi(z)=\mathrm{diag}(m_{1}(z),\dots,m_{n}(z))+d(z).

We can rewrite

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}(z)=\phi_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}=m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+d_{i}(z),

which yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon_{i}(z))

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{osc}\left(m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}\right)+2\|d(z)\|_{\infty}.

(A.8)

We now bound each term on the right-hand side separately.

We start with the first term on the right-hand side of (
A.8
). Since
|\frac{4R_{i}}{z^{2}}|\leq\frac{2}{3}<1
, the binomial series for
\sqrt{1-w}
is absolutely convergent at
w=\frac{4R_{i}}{z^{2}}
. Hence,

\sqrt{1-w}=1-2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{2k}{k}\left(\frac{w}{4}\right)^{k+1}=1-2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}C_{k}\left(\frac{w}{4}\right)^{k+1},

where
C_{k}=\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{2k}{k}
denotes the Catalan number. Applying this expansion

m_{i}(z)=\frac{z}{2R_{i}}\left(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{4R_{i}}{z^{2}}}\right),

we get

\displaystyle m_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{2R_{i}}\left[1-\left(1-2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}C_{k}\left(\frac{4R_{i}/z^{2}}{4}\right)^{k+1}\right)\right]=\frac{z}{R_{i}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{R_{i}^{k+1}}{z^{2k+2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{R_{i}^{k}}{z^{2k+1}}:=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}+v_{i}(z)

(A.9)

and thus

\displaystyle\Phi(z)=\frac{1}{z}I+\frac{1}{z^{3}}\mathcal{R}+\mathrm{diag}(v_{i}(z))_{1\leq i\leq n}+d(z).

(A.10)

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}\left(m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{i,j}|v_{i}(z)-v_{j}(z)|

\displaystyle=\max_{i,j}\left|\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{R_{i}^{k}-R_{j}^{k}}{z^{2k+1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{kR_{\max}^{k-1}}{|z|^{2k+1}}\right)\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}),

where we used

|R_{i}^{k}-R_{j}^{k}|=|(R_{i}-R_{j})(R_{i}^{k-1}+R_{i}^{k-2}R_{j}+\dots+R_{j}^{k-1})|\leq\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})\cdot kR_{\max}^{k-1}.

Since
R_{\max}/|z|^{2}\leq 1/6
by our assumption, we continue to have

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}\left(m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{5}}\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}kC_{k}\frac{R_{\max}^{k-2}}{|z|^{2(k-2)}}\right)\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{kC_{k}}{6^{k-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{11R_{\max}}{|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

(A.11)

In the last step, we used
\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{kC_{k}}{6^{k-2}}\leq 11
. This can be derived by taking the generating function of the Catalan numbers

\displaystyle c(x)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}C_{k}x^{k}=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-4x}}{2x}.

(A.12)

The sum is
S=\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}kC_{k}x^{k-2}
evaluated at
x=1/6
. Differentiating the generating function gives
c^{\prime}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kC_{k}x^{k-1}=1+xS
. Therefore, the sum is exactly given by
S=\frac{c^{\prime}(x)-1}{x}
. Taking the derivative yields
c^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1-2x-\sqrt{1-4x}}{2x^{2}\sqrt{1-4x}}
. Evaluating this at
x=1/6
gives
c^{\prime}(1/6)=12\sqrt{3}-18
. Substituting this back into the sum formula yields the exact value:

\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{kC_{k}}{6^{k-2}}=6(12\sqrt{3}-19)\leq 11.

Furthermore, we obtain the following bound for later estimates:

\displaystyle|m_{i}(z)-m_{j}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|R_{i}-R_{j}|}{|z|^{3}}+\mathrm{osc}\left(m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}}{|z|^{3}}+\frac{11R_{\max}}{|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{6}\right)\frac{\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}}{|z|^{3}}=\frac{7}{6}\frac{\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}}{|z|^{3}}.

(A.13)

In the last inequality, we applied
R_{\max}/|z|^{2}\leq 1/6.

Next, we turn to the second term on the right-hand side of (
A.8
). For each
i
, we have

\displaystyle|d_{i}(z)|=|\phi_{i}(z)-m_{i}(z)|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)}-\frac{1}{z-R_{i}m_{i}(z)}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{|R_{i}m_{i}(z)-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)|}{|z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)|\cdot|z-R_{i}m_{i}(z)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{|z|^{2}}\Big|R_{i}m_{i}(z)-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)\Big|.

(A.14)

For the last step above, observe that
|z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)|\geq\frac{3}{4}|z|
by Lemma
5.1
(i). In addition, we claim that

|z-R_{i}m_{i}(z)|\geq\frac{2|z|}{3}.

To see this, from (
A.7
), we have
|z-R_{i}m_{i}(z)|=\frac{1}{|m_{i}(z)|}.

Using the series expansion of
m_{i}(z)
in (
A.2
) and
R_{\max}/|z|^{2}\leq 1/6
, we get

\displaystyle|m_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{1}{|z|}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}C_{k}\Bigr(\frac{R_{i}}{|z|^{2}}\Bigl)^{k}\leq\frac{1}{|z|}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{k}}{6^{k}}=\frac{c(1/6)}{|z|}<\frac{1.5}{|z|},

where
c(x)
is defined in (
A.12
). The claim is proved.

It remains to estimate
|R_{i}m_{i}(z)-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)|
. We write

\displaystyle\Big|R_{i}m_{i}(z)-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)\Big|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(m_{i}(z)-m_{j}(z))+\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(m_{j}(z)-\phi_{j}(z))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|m_{i}(z)-m_{j}(z)|+R_{\max}\|d(z)\|_{\infty}.

Plugging in (
A.2
), we further have

\displaystyle|R_{i}m_{i}(z)-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z)|\leq\frac{7}{6}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}+R_{\max}\|d(z)\|_{\infty}

and thus from (
A.2
),

\displaystyle\|d(z)\|_{\infty}=\max_{i}|d_{i}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{|z|^{2}}\left(\frac{7}{6}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}+R_{\max}\|d(z)\|_{\infty}\right)

(A.15)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7}{3}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}+\frac{1}{3}\|d(z)\|_{\infty}.

(A.16)

Rearranging terms, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|d(z)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{7R_{\max}}{2|z|^{5}}\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}.

(A.17)

Finally, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon_{i}(z))\leq\mathrm{osc}\left(m_{i}(z)-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{R_{i}}{z^{3}}\right)+2\|d(z)\|_{\infty}\leq 14.5\frac{R_{\max}}{z^{5}}\mathrm{osc}{(\mathcal{R})}.

A.3
Proof of Proposition
5.1

By part (ii) of Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{z^{3}}+\epsilon_{j}(z),\qquad|\epsilon_{j}(z)|=|u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(z)u_{j}|\leq\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{z^{5}},

(A.18)

for all real
z\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. For brevity, denote
f(z)=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}
and thus
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(z)=1-\lambda_{j}f(z)
.

We first show that
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}
has a unique zero. Note that
\mathcal{V}_{jj}\geq 0
. At
z_{0}:=3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
,

f(z_{0})=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z_{0})u_{j}\geq\frac{1}{z_{0}}-\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{z_{0}^{5}}=\left(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{45}{8\cdot 3^{5}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{R_{\max}}}=\frac{67}{216}\frac{1}{\sqrt{R_{\max}}},

hence

\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(z_{0})\leq 1-\frac{67}{216}\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\sqrt{R_{\max}}}<0

since
\lambda_{j}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. On the other hand,
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(z)\to 1
as
z\to\infty
. Next, by part (ii) of Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(z)=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\prime}(z)u_{j}=-\frac{1}{z^{2}}-\frac{3\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{z^{4}}+\epsilon_{j}^{\prime}(z),\qquad|\epsilon_{j}^{\prime}(z)|=|u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon^{\prime}(z)u_{j}|\leq\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{z^{6}}.

(A.19)

Therefore, on the interval
[3\sqrt{R_{\max}},\infty)
,

\widehat{\alpha}_{j}^{\prime}(z)=-f^{\prime}(z)\geq\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{1}{z^{2}}-\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{z^{6}}\right)>0

since

\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{z^{6}}\leq\frac{243}{5}\frac{1}{81z^{2}}=\frac{243}{405}\frac{1}{z^{2}}<\frac{1}{z^{2}}.

Therefore,
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}
is strictly increasing, and has a unique and simple real zero
\vartheta_{j}\in[3\sqrt{R_{\max}},\infty)
.

Observe that at
z=\lambda_{j}/2\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
and
z=2\lambda_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(\lambda_{j}/2)=1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\lambda_{j}/2)u_{j}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{2}{\lambda_{j}}-\frac{45}{8}\frac{2^{5}R_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{5}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-1+\frac{5}{36}<0

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{j}(2\lambda_{j})=1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(2\lambda_{j})u_{j}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{1}{2\lambda_{j}}+\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{2^{5}\lambda_{j}^{5}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{45}{9216}>0.

Since
\widehat{\alpha}_{j}
is strictly increasing, we have

\frac{\lambda_{j}}{2}\leq\vartheta_{j}\leq 2\lambda_{j}.

Now we locate
\vartheta_{j}
. Note that
f(\vartheta_{j})=\lambda_{j}^{-1}
. We introduce a scalar comparison function

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{V},j}(z)=\frac{z-\sqrt{z^{2}-4\mathcal{V}_{jj}}}{2\mathcal{V}_{jj}}

(A.20)

which satisfies the quadratic equation

\displaystyle m(z)=\frac{1}{z-\mathcal{V}_{jj}m(z)}.

(A.21)

This function approximates
f(z)=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}
by noting
\mathcal{V}_{jj}=\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}R_{i}
and the definition of
\phi_{i}(z)
in (
5.1
).

Denote

x_{j}:=\lambda_{j}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}.

Then
x_{j}
is a zero of
1-\lambda_{j}m_{\mathcal{V},j}(z)=0
. Indeed, plugging
m_{\mathcal{V},j}(z)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}}
into (
A.21
), we get

\lambda_{j}=z-\mathcal{V}_{jj}m_{\mathcal{V},j}(z)=z-\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}
and thus
z=\lambda_{j}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}.

Next, we expand
m_{\mathcal{V},j}(z)
as a Catalan series the same as (
A.2
) at
z=x_{j}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}}=m_{\mathcal{V},j}(x_{j})=\frac{1}{x_{j}}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{x_{j}^{3}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}^{k}}{x_{j}^{2k+1}}.

(A.22)

To estimate
|\vartheta_{j}-x_{j}|
, we first show that
f(x_{j})=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(x_{j})u_{j}\approx\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}}=f(\vartheta_{j})
. Then we apply the mean value theorem to bound
|\vartheta_{j}-x_{j}|
.

In the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we have the expansion of
\Phi(z)
given in (
A.10
). Hence,

\displaystyle f(x_{j})=u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(x_{j})u_{j}=\frac{1}{x_{j}}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{x_{j}^{3}}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}C_{k}\frac{\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}R_{i}^{k}}{x_{j}^{2k+1}}+u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}d(x_{j})u_{j}.

Subtracting with (
A.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle|f(x_{j})-f(\vartheta_{j})|=|f(x_{j})-\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}}|\leq\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{C_{k}}{x_{j}^{2k+1}}\left|\mathcal{V}_{jj}^{k}-\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}R_{i}^{k}\right|+\|d(x_{j})\|_{\infty}.

Note that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{V}_{jj}^{k}-\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}R_{i}^{k}\right|\leq\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}\left|\mathcal{V}_{jj}^{k}-R_{i}^{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq kR_{\max}^{k}\sum_{i}u_{j}(i)^{2}\left|\mathcal{V}_{jj}-R_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq kR_{\max}^{k}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Following the same step as (
A.2
) and combining the bound on
\|d\|_{\infty}
in (
A.17
), we arrive at

\displaystyle|f(x_{j})-f(\vartheta_{j})|\leq 11\frac{R_{\max}}{x_{j}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\frac{7}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{x_{j}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})\leq 14.5\frac{R_{\max}}{\lambda_{j}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Finally, by mean value theorem,
|f(x_{j})-f(\vartheta_{j})|=|x_{j}-\vartheta_{j}|\cdot|f^{\prime}(w)|
for some
w
between
x_{j}
and
\vartheta_{j}
. Since
\lambda_{j}/2\leq\vartheta_{j}\leq 2\lambda_{j}
and
x_{j}\leq 2\lambda_{j}
, we have
w\leq 2\lambda_{j}
. By (
A.19
), we get

f^{\prime}(w)\geq\frac{1}{w^{2}}-\frac{243}{5}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{w^{6}}\geq\frac{1}{w^{2}}\left(1-\frac{243}{5\cdot 81}\right)=\frac{2}{5w^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{10\lambda_{j}^{2}}.

Rearranging yields that

\left|\lambda_{j}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{jj}}{\lambda_{j}}-\vartheta_{j}\right|=|x_{j}-\vartheta_{j}|\leq 145\frac{R_{\max}}{\lambda_{j}^{3}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

The proof is now complete.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
5.4

We apply Corollary 3.12 from
[
14
]
with a standard truncation technique. Denote

L_{n}:=K\sigma_{\max}((D+4)\log n)^{1/2}.

By a union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|>L_{n})\leq n^{2}\cdot 2\exp(-(L_{n}/K)^{2})=2n^{-(D+2)}.

We split
E
into big and small parts according to
L_{n}
. Namely, we write
E=Z+M+B
, where we define these symmetric matrices via

Z_{ij}=E_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|\leq L_{n}\}}-\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|\leq L_{n}\}}),\,M_{ij}=\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|\leq L_{n}\}}),\,B_{ij}=E_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|>L_{n}\}}.

Note that if
\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\leq L_{n}
, then
B=0
. We work on the event
\mathcal{E}=\{\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\leq L_{n}\}
below and previous calculations suggest that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-2n^{-(D+2)}.

Next, we bound
\|M\|
. Note that since
E_{ij}
has mean 0,
M_{ij}=-\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|>L_{n}\}})
. Therefore,

|M_{ij}|\leq\mathbb{E}(|E_{ij}|\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|>L_{n}\}})=\int_{L_{n}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(|E_{ij}|\geq t)\,dt\leq 2L_{n}n^{-(D+4)}

since
\mathbb{P}(|E_{ij}|>L_{n})\leq 2n^{-(D+4)}.
Then we have

\|M\|\leq\max_{i}\sum_{j}|M_{ij}|\leq 2L_{n}n^{-(D+3)}.

Finally, for the bound on
\|Z\|
, we apply Corollary 3.12 from
[
14
]
. Note that
Z_{ij}
’s have mean 0 and
|Z_{ij}|\leq 2L_{n}
. Also,
\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}^{2})\leq\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}^{2})=\sigma_{ij}^{2}
. Applying of
[
14
, Corollary 3.12 ]
, together with a standard symmetrization (as in the proof of
[
14
, Corollary 3.3 ]
), yields that for
t>0,

\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z\|\geq 2\sqrt{2}(1+\epsilon)\tilde{\sigma}+t\right)\leq n\exp(-t^{2}/(c_{\epsilon}L_{n})^{2}).

We choose
\epsilon
such that
2\sqrt{2}(1+\epsilon)=2.9
and denote
c_{\epsilon}=c
. The parameter
\tilde{\sigma}=\max_{i}\sqrt{\sum_{j}\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}^{2})}=\sqrt{\max_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}}=\sqrt{R_{\max}}
by our supposition. Choosing
t=cL_{n}\sqrt{(D+3)\log n}
, we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z\|\geq 2.9\sqrt{R_{\max}}+cL_{n}\sqrt{(D+3)\log n}\right)\leq n^{-(D+2)}.

Combining the above estimates, we have that with probability at least
1-3n^{-(D+2)}
,

\displaystyle\|E\|\leq\|Z\|+\|M\|+\|B\|

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{n}n^{-(D+3)}+1.5\sqrt{R_{\max}}+cL_{n}\sqrt{(D+3)\log n}

\displaystyle\leq 2.9\sqrt{R_{\max}}+cK\sigma_{\max}(D+4)\log n.

Appendix B
Proofs of Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
7.2

B.1
Proof of Proposition
7.1

Let
Q=I-UU^{\mathrm{T}}
be the orthogonal projection onto the null space of
A
. Set
P_{J}=U_{J}U_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}
where
J=[r]\setminus[k]
and
U_{J}=(u_{k+1},\dots,u_{r})
. Then

P_{U_{k}^{\perp}}=P_{J}+Q.

We first prove (
7.2
) in part (i). Starting from
\|\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\|=\|P_{U_{k}^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|
(see, for instance,
[
25
, Exercises VII. 1. 9-1.11]
), we have

\displaystyle\|\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\|=\|(P_{J}+Q)P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{J}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|+\|QP_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{F}+\|QP_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|.

It remains to bound
\|QP_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|=\|Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|
. From the spectral decomposition of
\widetilde{A}
, we have
(A+E)\widetilde{U}_{k}=\widetilde{U}_{k}\tilde{\Lambda}_{k}
. Multiplying by
Q
on the left and noting that
QA=0
, we obtain
QE\widetilde{U}_{k}=Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
. Since
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
is invertible, we have

Q\widetilde{U}_{k}=QE\widetilde{U}_{k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}.

To see that
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}
is indeed invertible, note that by Weyl’s inequality, for every
1\leq s\leq k
,

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}\lambda_{s}\geq\lambda_{s}+\|E\|\geq\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\geq\lambda_{s}-\|E\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{s},

(B.1)

where the last inequality follows from our assumption
\lambda_{s}\geq\lambda_{k}\geq 2\|E\|
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|QP_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|=\|QE\widetilde{U}_{k}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\|\leq\|QE\widetilde{U}_{k}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{\|E\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}\leq 2\frac{\|E\|}{{\lambda}_{k}}.

(B.2)

Now we turn to part (ii). Note that (
7.3
) is given in Eq. (10) of
[
83
]
. It remains to prove (
7.1
). It follows from the decomposition
\widetilde{u}_{s}=P_{U_{k}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+P_{J}\widetilde{u}_{s}+Q\widetilde{u}_{s}
for each
1\leq s\leq k
that

\widetilde{U}_{k}=P_{U_{k}}\widetilde{U}_{k}+P_{J}\widetilde{U}_{k}+Q\widetilde{U}_{k}

and thus

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{k}-P_{U_{k}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}=\|P_{J}\widetilde{U}_{k}+Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{J}U_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}+\|Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|U\|_{2,\infty}\cdot\|U_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{F}+\|Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}.

To finish the proof, we show that

\displaystyle\|Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{13}{2}\sqrt{k}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}+4\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{k}\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left\|e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(z)U\right\|^{2}}.

For
1\leq s\leq k
, from the
(A+E)\widetilde{u}_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\widetilde{u}_{s}
, we have
A\widetilde{u}_{s}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}-E)\widetilde{u}_{s}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}_{s}=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}=\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}

(B.3)

and for any canonical vector
e_{l}
, we obtain

\displaystyle e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\widetilde{U}_{k}=\big(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}\big)_{s\in[k]}+\big(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}\big)_{s\in[k]}:=\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(1)}+\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(2)}.

It follows that

\|Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\widetilde{U}_{k}\|\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\big(\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(1)}\|+\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(2)}\|\big).

We bound
\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(1)}\|
and
\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(2)}\|
on the right hand side. We start from

\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(1)}\|=\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{k}\big(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}\big)^{2}}.

For each
l\in[n]
, observe that

\displaystyle|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}|=|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}|\leq\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\cdot\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|.

(B.4)

Note that

\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}.

This is because from
(A+E)\widetilde{u}_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\widetilde{u}_{s}
, we have
A\widetilde{u}_{s}=U\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}I-E)\widetilde{u}_{s}
. Multiplying
U^{\mathrm{T}}
on each side, we further get
\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}=U^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}-E)\widetilde{u}_{s}
and

\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|\leq(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}+\|E\|)\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}

by (
B.1
) and our assumption
\lambda_{s}\geq\lambda_{k}\geq 2\|E\|
.

Next, we estimate
\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|
. Since
\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{s}\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, we plug in the expansion of
\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})
in (
5.4
) to get

e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U=e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\left(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}I_{n}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\right)U=e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\left(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\right)U.

Note that both
\mathcal{R}
and
\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})
are diagonal matrices. Indeed, for a general diagonal matrix
D=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})
,

e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}QDU=e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-UU^{\mathrm{T}})DU=e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}DU-e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}U(U^{\mathrm{T}}DU)=e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}U(d_{l}I-U^{\mathrm{T}}DU)

due to
e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}D=d_{l}e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}
and hence,

\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}QDU\|\leq\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}U\|(|d_{l}|+\|U^{\mathrm{T}}DU\|)\leq 2\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|D\|.

Using the error estimate in (
5.4
), we have

\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\leq 2\|U\|_{2,\infty}\left(\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{45}{8}\frac{R_{\max}^{2}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\right)\leq\frac{13}{4}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}},

where we used
\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{s}\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
from (
B.1
) and our assumption
\lambda_{k}\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}.
Combining the above estimates, we continue from (
B.4
) to achieve

|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}|\leq\frac{13}{2}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{2}}\leq\frac{13}{2}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}.

Thus

\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(1)}\|=\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{k}\big(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}\big)^{2}}\leq\frac{13}{2}\sqrt{k}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}.

The estimate for

\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(2)}\|=\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{k}\big(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}\big)^{2}}

is simpler. Using an estimate similar to (
B.4
), we have

\displaystyle|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}|=|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}|

\displaystyle\leq\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\cdot\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|.

Thus

\|\mathtt{q}_{l}^{(2)}\|\leq 4\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{k}\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left\|e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(z)U\right\|^{2}}.

Combining all the above estimates, we complete the proof.

B.2
Proof of Proposition
7.2

Fix
s\in[k]
. Recall that
J=[r]\setminus[k]
. We partition
J
into two parts

\mathcal{J}:=\{k+1,\dots,r_{+}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{N}:=\{r_{+}+1,\dots,r\}.

Then

\|U_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|^{2}=\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|^{2}+\|U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|^{2}.

We estimate each term separately.

Step 1. Bound on
\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|
.
Set
\mathcal{I}=[k]\cup\{r_{+}+1,\dots,r\}=[r]\setminus\mathcal{J}
. We then have the decomposition

A=U\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}=U_{\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}+U_{\mathcal{I}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}.

Multiplying both sides of (
B.3
) by
U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}
from the left yields

\displaystyle U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}

\displaystyle=U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})A\widetilde{u}_{s}

(B.5)

\displaystyle=U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\cdot U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\cdot\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s},

(B.6)

where we have used the decomposition of
A
. Isolating the term
U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}
gives

\displaystyle\left(I-U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\right)U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}=U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\cdot\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}.

(B.7)

Denote

L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}):=I-U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}.

We first bound the smallest singular value
s_{\min}(L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))
. We split

\displaystyle U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}(u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}+\mathrm{Off}(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{diag}(u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}+Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})

and rewrite

\displaystyle L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})

\displaystyle=I-\mathrm{diag}(u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}-Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{diag}\left(1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j}\right)_{j\in\mathcal{J}}-Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}.

Then

\displaystyle s_{\min}(L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))\geq\frac{\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}-\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|

(B.8)

with
\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\min_{j\in\mathcal{J}}|1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j}|
defined in (
7.5
).

Now we bound
\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|
on the right-hand side of (
B.8
). Since
\widetilde{{\lambda}_{s}}\geq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, by the second-order expansion of
\Phi
in (
A.5
), we have

\displaystyle Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})=\mathrm{Off}(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}})=\mathrm{Off}\left(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{J}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\right)+\mathrm{Off}\left(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\right).

Note, by orthogonality, that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Off}\left(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right\|=\left\|\mathrm{Off}\left(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\big(\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})-c^{*}I\big)U_{\mathcal{J}}\right)\right\|\leq\|\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})-c^{*}I\|=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})),

where we select
c^{*}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})+\min_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\right)
.
It follows, by applying Proposition
5.2
, that

\displaystyle\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{L}}\|\leq\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\|\mathrm{Off}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{J}})\|+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}\lambda_{k+1}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Our assumption implies that

\frac{\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}\geq 2\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{L}}\|

and hence, from (
B.8
), we get

s_{\min}(L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}.

Next, we turn to the term on the right-hand side of (
B.7
). Applying the triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\cdot\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|

(B.9)

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\|\cdot\|\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|+\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\cdot\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|.

(B.10)

For the bound on
\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|
, starting from
(A+E)\widetilde{u}_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\widetilde{u}_{s}
, we have
U\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}I-E)\widetilde{u}_{s}
. Multiplying
U^{\mathrm{T}}
from the left on both sides and taking the norms yield

\|\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|=\|U^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}I-E)\widetilde{u}_{s}\|\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}+\|E\|\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}.

The last step follows from (
B.1
) and our assumption
\lambda_{s}\geq 2\|E\|
. Similarly, we also have

\|\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}}U_{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}.

The right-hand side of (
B.7
) is bounded by

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\left(\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\|+\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right).

It remains to estimate
\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\|
. We plug in the second-order expansion of
\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})
in (
A.5
). By the orthogonality of eigenvectors, we get

\displaystyle U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}U_{\mathcal{I}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})-c^{*}I\right)U_{\mathcal{I}},

where
c^{*}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\max_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})+\min_{i}\varepsilon_{i}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\right).
By Proposition
5.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{I}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\|\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})-c^{*}I\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))\leq\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Therefore, the right-hand side of can be bounded by

2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\left(\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right).

Finally, combining the above estimates, we continue from (
B.7
) to achieve the bound

\displaystyle\|U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\left(\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right)}{s_{\min}(L_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}\left(\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{2}\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right).

Step 2. Bound on
\|U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|
.
Denote the index set
\mathcal{K}:=[r]\setminus\mathcal{N}
. We decompose

A=U_{\mathcal{N}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{N}}U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}+U_{\mathcal{K}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{K}}U_{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{T}}.

Following the same derivation of (
B.7
), we have

\displaystyle\left(I-U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{N}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{N}}\right)U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}=U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{K}}\cdot\Lambda_{\mathcal{K}}U_{\mathcal{K}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}+U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\cdot\Lambda U^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}.

(B.11)

Denote

\widehat{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}):=I-U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{N}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{N}}.

We first bound
s_{\min}\big(\widehat{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\big)
. Since
\Lambda_{\mathcal{N}}
contains only negative eigenvalues, set
D_{\mathcal{N}}:=\mathrm{diag}(|\lambda_{l}|)_{l\in\mathcal{N}}=-\Lambda_{\mathcal{N}}\succ 0
. Denote
Q_{\mathcal{N}}:=U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{N}}
for simplicity. Since
\phi_{i}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})>0
,
Q_{\mathcal{N}}\succ 0
. Rewrite

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})=I+Q_{\mathcal{N}}D_{\mathcal{N}}=Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\left(I+Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}D_{\mathcal{N}}Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{-1/2}\right)Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}.

Observe that
I+Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}D_{\mathcal{N}}Q_{\mathcal{N}}^{-1/2}\succeq I
. We get

\displaystyle s_{\min}\big(\widehat{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\big)\geq\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\min}(Q_{\mathcal{N}})}{\lambda_{\max}(Q_{\mathcal{N}})}}.

To bound the eigenvalues of
Q_{\mathcal{N}}
, note that for any unit vector
v
,
v^{\mathrm{T}}Q_{\mathcal{N}}v=(U_{\mathcal{N}}v)^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})(U_{\mathcal{N}}v).
Combining Lemma
5.1
(i), we get

\frac{1}{2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}\leq\min_{i}\phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\leq\lambda_{\min}(Q_{\mathcal{N}})\leq\lambda_{\max}(Q_{\mathcal{N}})\leq\max_{i}\phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\leq\frac{3}{2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}

and thus

s_{\min}\big(\widehat{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\big)\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{3}}.

By the same estimation as in
Step 1
, we bound the right-hand side of (
B.11
) by

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\left(\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{K}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\|U_{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{3}\left(\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{K}}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{2}}+\frac{13}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{4}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})+\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U\|\right).

This completes the proof.

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
8.1

Before we prove Proposition
8.1
, we introduce the necessary notation and a cumulant expansion formula to be used in the proof (adapted from
[
57
]
).

For any matrix
F\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
and vectors
v,w\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we write

F_{vw}:=v^{\mathrm{T}}Fw,\qquad F_{iw}:=e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Fw,\qquad F_{vj}:=v^{\mathrm{T}}Fe_{j},\qquad F_{ij}:=e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Fe_{j},

where
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}\}
denotes the standard basis of
