\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Moreover, for
1\leq i,j\leq n
define

\Delta^{ij}:=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\bigl(e_{i}e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}+e_{j}e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}\bigr),

(C.1)

and for a differentiable function
f=f(H)
of a symmetric matrix
H=(H_{ij})
(with
\{H_{ij}:i\leq j\}
treated as independent variables) set

\partial_{ij}f\;:=\;\frac{\partial}{\partial H_{ij}}f(H)\;=\;\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right|_{t=0}\,f\bigl(H+t\,\Delta_{ij}\bigr).

Since there is potential ambiguity between
\bigl|\partial_{ij}^{\,r}(G_{vw})\bigr|
and
\bigl|(\partial_{ij}^{\,r}G)_{vw}\bigr|
, we explicitly note that

\partial_{ij}^{\,r}\bigl(v^{\mathrm{T}}Gw\bigr)\;=\;v^{\mathrm{T}}\bigl(\partial_{ij}^{\,r}G\bigr)w,

and throughout the proof we therefore write
\bigl|\partial_{ij}^{\,r}G_{vw}\bigr|
with no confusion.

L
EMMA
C.1

(Cumulant expansion, Lemma 2.4 from
[
57
]
)
.

Let
h
be a real random variable with finite moments of all orders. For
k\geq 1
define the
k
-th cumulant by

\mathcal{C}_{k}(h):=(-\mathrm{i})^{k}\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}^{k}}{\mathrm{d}t^{k}}\log\mathbb{E}\bigl[e^{\mathrm{i}th}\bigr]\right|_{t=0}.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
be smooth, and write
f^{(k)}
for its
k
-th derivative. Fix
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\bigl[hf(h)\bigr]=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\frac{1}{k!}\,\mathcal{C}_{k+1}(h)\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[f^{(k)}(h)\bigr]+\mathtt{R}_{\ell+1},

(C.2)

where the remainder
R_{\ell+1}
satisfies, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle|\mathtt{R}_{\ell+1}|\leq

\displaystyle\ 2^{\ell}(\ell+2)\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{|x|\leq|h|}\bigl|f^{(\ell+1)}(x)\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl[|h|^{2\ell+4}\mathbf{1}(|h|>t)\bigr]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+2^{\ell}(\ell+2)\cdot\mathbb{E}|h|^{\ell+2}\cdot\sup_{|x|\leq t}\bigl|f^{(\ell+1)}(x)\bigr|.

(C.3)

Now we provide the proof. Define

L:=\mathrm{diag}(l_{1},\dots,l_{n})\quad\text{with}\quad l_{k}:=\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sigma_{ks}^{2}G_{ss}.

(C.4)

The main technical task is to establish:

P
ROPOSITION
C.1
.

Fix
D>0
. Let
v,w\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be deterministic unit vectors, and let

z\in\mathbb{C}
satisfy
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
.

(i)
Under the general sub-Gaussian regime, with probability at least

1-6n^{-D-4}
,

\left|v^{\mathrm{T}}(E-L)Gw\right|\leq C(D+6)^{3/2}\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}\log^{2}n}{|z|}+Cn^{-500},

where
C\geq 1
is an absolute constant.

(ii)
Under the bounded sparse-entry regime, namely if

\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\leq c_{0}K\sigma_{\max}\quad\text{almost surely}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\beta\leq\beta_{0},

then, with probability at least
1-6n^{-D-4}
,

\left|v^{\mathrm{T}}(E-L)Gw\right|\leq C(D+6)(c_{0}+\beta_{0})\frac{K\sigma_{\max}\log n}{|z|},

where
C>0
is an absolute constant.

The key to proving Proposition
C.1
is the following moment estimates, whose proof is deferred to Section
C.1
. For brevity, we denote

\mathcal{D}:=(E-L)G.

(C.5)

and bound the moments of

\mathcal{D}_{vw}=v^{\mathrm{T}}(E-L)Gw.

We define a regime-dependent truncation parameter

\mathtt{L}_{n}:=\begin{cases}100\sqrt{D+6}K\sigma_{\max}\log n,&\text{in the general sub-Gaussian regime},\\
c_{0}K\sigma_{\max},&\text{in the bounded sparse-entry regime}.\end{cases}

(C.6)

Define

\Omega_{n}^{0}:=\left\{\|E\|\leq 3\sqrt{R_{\max}},\quad\max_{a,b}|E_{ab}|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}\right\}

and a slightly larger event

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}:=\left\{\|E\|\leq 3.5\sqrt{R_{\max}},\quad\max_{a,b}|E_{ab}|\leq 2\mathtt{L}_{n}\right\}.

(C.7)

Let
\chi=\chi(E)\in[0,1]
be a smooth cutoff such that

\chi(E)=1\quad\text{on }\Omega_{n}^{0},\qquad\chi(E)=0\quad\text{outside }\Omega_{n}.

Our main technical estimate is the following bound:

L
EMMA
C.2
.

For any integer
p\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E))\leq

\displaystyle CK\beta\sum_{m=1}^{3}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\chi(E)\right]

\displaystyle+CK^{2}\beta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\chi(E)\right]+\varepsilon_{p}

for an absolute constant
C\geq 1
. Here, we define

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{p}:=C^{p}p^{3}\exp\bigl(-5000(D+6)(\log n)^{2}\bigr)\dfrac{(\beta K\sigma_{\max})^{2}}{|z|^{2}}

(C.8)

in the general sub-Gaussian regime and
\varepsilon_{p}:=0
in the bounded sparse-entry regime.

Using this lemma, we first obtain a bound on

\mathtt{Z}_{p}:=\left(\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E))\right)^{1/2p}.

By the Hölder inequality, for any
q\leq 2p
,

\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{q}\chi(E)]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E)\right)^{\frac{q}{2p}}=\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{q}.

The same argument gives

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{q}\chi(E)\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}\right)^{2p}\chi(E)\right]\right)^{\frac{q}{2p}}.

Note that by the Minkowski inequality,

\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}\right)^{2p}\chi(E)\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\leq\left(\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E)\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}=\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}.

We get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}\right)^{q}\chi(E)\right]\leq\left(\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{q}.

Therefore, Lemma
C.2
implies

\displaystyle\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}\leq

\displaystyle CK\beta\sum_{m=1}^{3}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\left(\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}

\displaystyle+CK^{2}\beta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\left(\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}+\varepsilon_{p},

(C.9)

where
\varepsilon_{p}
is defined in (
C.8
).

We claim that

\mathtt{Z}_{p}\leq C\left(p\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}+p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}+\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{2}+\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}\right).

(C.10)

To see this, consider the case where
\mathtt{Z}_{p}\leq 88p\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}+2\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}
. In this regime, (
C.10
) holds trivially. Now, let us assume
\mathtt{Z}_{p}>88p\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}
and
\mathtt{Z}_{p}>2\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}
. Thus,
\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\leq(1+\frac{1}{4p})\mathtt{Z}_{p}
and for
1\leq m\leq 4
,

\left(\mathtt{Z}_{p}+22\frac{\mathtt{L_{n}}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{4p}\right)^{2p}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}\leq e^{1/2}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}.

Moreover, the last term in (
C
) satisfies
\varepsilon_{p}\leq 2^{-2p}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}
and can be absorbed into the left-hand side. Hence, (
C
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{3}CK\beta p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}+\sum_{m=1}^{4}CK^{2}\beta^{2}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}

(C.11)

for an absolute constant
C\geq 1
. Next, we apply Young’s inequality for
\mathtt{B}\cdot\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}
. Let
\delta>0
be a small constant to be specified later. Since

\displaystyle\mathtt{B}\cdot\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m}=(\delta\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m})\cdot\frac{\mathtt{B}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\delta\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p-m})^{2p/(2p-m)}}{2p/(2p-m)}+\frac{(\mathtt{B}/\delta)^{2p/m}}{2p/m}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta^{2p/(2p-m)}}{2p/(2p-m)}\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}+\frac{(1/\delta)^{2p/m}}{2p/m}\mathtt{B}^{\frac{2p}{m}}.

Choose
\delta=1/100
. Then
\frac{\delta^{2p/(2p-m)}}{2p/(2p-m)}<1/10
for
1\leq m\leq 4
. We absorb the
\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}
terms to the left-hand side of (
C.11
). Continuing from (
C.11
), we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{Z}_{p}^{2p}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{3}\left(CK\beta p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\right)^{\frac{2p}{m}}+\sum_{m=1}^{4}\left(CK^{2}\beta^{2}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{2p}{m}}.

Taking the
2p
-th roots on both sides yields

\displaystyle\mathtt{Z}_{p}\leq C\max_{1\leq m\leq 3}\left(K\beta p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}+C\max_{1\leq m\leq 4}\left(K^{2}\beta^{2}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}.

Since
K\beta\geq 1
,
p\geq 3
, and
|z|\geq\sigma_{\max}
, we have for
1\leq m\leq 3
,

\left(K\beta p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}=(K\beta)^{\frac{1}{m}}p^{\frac{m-1}{m}}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\leq p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}

and for
2\leq m\leq 4
,

\displaystyle(K\beta)^{\frac{2}{m}}p^{\frac{m-1}{m}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{1+\frac{1}{m}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)\cdot(K\beta)^{\frac{2-m}{m}}\cdot p^{\frac{m-1}{m}}\cdot\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}

\displaystyle\leq p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}.

The above inequality simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathtt{Z}_{p}\leq C\left(p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}+\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{2}\right).

Combining this bound with the exceptional case proves (
C.10
).

Now we prove Proposition
8.1
. Let us first consider the general sub-Gaussian regime. By the union bound and our sub-Gaussian tail assumption,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{a,b}|E_{ab}|>2\mathtt{L}_{n}\right)\leq\sum_{a,b}\mathbb{P}(|E_{ab}|>\mathtt{L}_{n})\leq 2n^{2}\exp\left(-10^{4}(D+6)(\log n)^{2}\right)\leq 2n^{-D-4}.

Moreover, by Lemma
5.4
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\|E\|>3\sqrt{R_{\max}})\leq 3n^{-D-4}

by selecting the constant
C_{0}
in Assumption
1.2
sufficiently large.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{n}^{0})^{c}\big)\leq 5n^{-D-4}.

From the decomposition
|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|=|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{0}}+|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\mathbf{1}_{(\Omega_{n}^{0})^{c}}
and
\chi(E)=1
on
\Omega_{n}^{0}
, we have
|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{0}}\leq|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\chi(E)
. Thus

|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\leq|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\chi(E)+|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\mathbf{1}_{(\Omega_{n}^{0})^{c}}.

It follows that

\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\geq t)\leq\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\chi(E)\geq t)+\mathbb{P}\big((\Omega_{n}^{0})^{c}\big).

Then by Markov’s inequality and
0\leq\chi\leq 1
, for any integer
p\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\geq t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E))}{t^{2p}}+5n^{-D-4}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathtt{Z}_{p}}{t}\right)^{2p}+5n^{-D-4}.

We choose
p=\lceil(D+4)\log n\rceil
and in view of (
C.10
),

t=e\cdot C\left(p\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}+p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}+\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{2}+\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}\right).

With this choice of
t
,

\left(\frac{\mathtt{Z}_{p}}{t}\right)^{2p}\leq e^{-2p}\leq\exp\big(-2(D+4)\log n\big)=n^{-2D-8}\leq n^{-D-4}.

It follows that

\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\geq t)\leq n^{-D-4}+5n^{-D-4}=6n^{-D-4}.

Finally, let us simplify the terms in
t
under the choice of
p
. For the term
\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}
, note that by Assumption
1.2
,
R_{\max}\leq n\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and
\sqrt{R_{\max}}\geq C_{0}(D+8)K\sigma_{\max}\log n.
We have

K\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{C_{0}(D+8)\log n}.

As discussed in Remark
1.1
,
\beta\leq C\sqrt{\log(eK)}\leq C\sqrt{\log n}
. Under our assumption
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
,
\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\leq C^{\prime}
. Thus

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{p}^{1/(2p)}

\displaystyle=C^{1/2}p^{3/(2p)}\exp\left(-\frac{10(D+6)\log^{2}n}{2p}\right)\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{5000(D+6)\log^{2}n}{2(D+5)\log n}\right)\leq C\exp(-500\log n)=Cn^{-500}.

Next, substituting
p\leq(D+5)\log n
and
\mathtt{L}_{n}=5\sqrt{D+6}K\sigma_{\max}\log n
, we also have

p\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\leq 5(D+6)^{3/2}\frac{K\sigma_{\max}\log^{2}n}{|z|}\quad\text{and}\quad p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\leq(D+5)\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}\log n}{|z|}.

Note that

\displaystyle p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\geq\left(\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right)^{2}

(C.12)

by selecting the constant
C_{0}
sufficiently large in Assumption
1.2
. Thus, we conclude that with probability at least
1-6n^{-D-4}
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(5(D+6)^{3/2}\frac{K\sigma_{\max}\log^{2}n}{|z|}+(D+5)\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}\log n}{|z|}+n^{-500}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(D+6)^{3/2}\frac{K\sigma_{\max}\log^{2}n}{|z|}+Cn^{-500}.

We now prove the bounded sparse-entry case. In this regime,

\mathtt{L}_{n}=c_{0}K\sigma_{\max}
and
\varepsilon_{p}=0
.
Thus from (
C.10
), together with
(
C.12
)
, we obtain

\mathtt{Z}_{p}\leq C\left(p\frac{c_{0}K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}+p\frac{\beta K\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\right).

Moreover, the entrywise truncation event is deterministic, i.e.,
\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}
almost surely.
Taking

p=\lceil(D+4)\log n\rceil
and applying the same Markov argument as above, together with the operator norm
event, yields

|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\leq C(c_{0}+\beta_{0})(D+6)\frac{K\sigma_{\max}\log n}{|z|}

with probability at least
1-6n^{-D-4}
. This proves part (ii) and completes the proof of Proposition
C.1
.

C.1
Proof of Lemma
C.2

We prove Lemma
C.2
in this section. Let

Q:=\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-1}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p}.

We rewrite

\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E)]=\mathbb{E}\left[(EG)_{vw}Q\chi(E)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LG\right)_{vw}Q\chi(E)\right].

(C.13)

We analyze the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.13
) using the cumulant expansion. For
1\leq i,j\leq n
, define the auxiliary functions

h_{ij}(E):=G_{jw}Q,\qquad f_{ij}^{\chi}(E):=h_{ij}(E)\chi(E).

Thus

\mathbb{E}\left[(EG)_{vw}Q\,\chi(E)\right]=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\,\mathbb{E}\left[E_{ij}f_{ij}^{\chi}(E)\right].

Let
\chi\equiv\chi(E)
. For
l\leq 3
, the product rule
gives

\partial_{ij}^{l}f_{ij}^{\chi}=\chi\cdot\partial_{ij}^{l}h_{ij}+\sum_{a=1}^{l}\binom{l}{a}(\partial_{ij}^{l-a}h_{ij})(\partial_{ij}^{a}\chi).

The first term on the right-hand side is the main term estimated below. The
remaining terms contain at least one derivative of the cutoff function. We choose

\chi
so that, for
1\leq a\leq 3
,

|\partial_{ij}^{a}\chi(E)|\leq C_{a}\mathtt{L}_{n}^{-a}.

These derivative terms are supported in

\Omega_{n}\setminus\Omega_{n}^{0}
. Using the derivative bounds proved below,
together with Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the high-probability estimate for

(\Omega_{n}^{0})^{c}
, the total contribution of the terms containing

\partial_{ij}^{a}\chi
,
a\geq 1
, is absorbed into an error

O(n^{-D-5})
, after increasing
C_{0}
if necessary.

In the estimates below, we write only the terms in which no derivative falls
on
\chi
. The terms involving derivatives of
\chi
are supported on

\Omega_{n}\setminus\Omega_{n}^{0}
. By the same derivative estimates used below
and the probability bound for this transition region, their total contribution
is negligible compared with the final high-probability bound. We therefore
record only the main terms in the moment estimate. The remainder

\varepsilon_{p}
below refers only to the tail part of the cumulant remainder;
in the bounded sparse-entry regime this tail part is zero. Therefore, to simplify notation, we suppress the cutoff
\chi(E)
from the main estimates. From this point on, we simply work with

f_{ij}(E):=G_{jw}Q

Since
E
is symmetric, the independent random variables are
E_{ij}
(
1\leq i\leq j\leq n)
. For
i<j
, we write
\partial_{ij}
for differentiation with respect to
E_{ij}=E_{ji}
, and we set
\partial_{ji}:=\partial_{ij}
. For the diagonal entries,

\partial_{ii}
denotes differentiation with respect to
E_{ii}
. With this convention, for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}G_{xy}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}(G_{xi}G_{jy}+G_{xj}G_{iy}),

(C.14)

Now, from

(EG)_{vw}Q=\sum_{i}v_{i}E_{ii}G_{iw}Q+\sum_{i<j}E_{ij}\bigl(v_{i}G_{jw}+v_{j}G_{iw}\bigr)Q=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}E_{ij}f_{ij}(E),

we apply the cumulant expansion Lemma
C.1
to each independent entry
E_{ij}
(
1\leq i\leq j\leq n)
with
l=2
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[(EG)_{vw}Q\right]:=X_{1}+X_{2}+\mathtt{R}_{3},

(C.15)

where for
k=1,2
,

X_{k}:=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{C}_{k+1}(E_{ij})\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{ij}^{k}f_{ij}(E)\right].

(C.16)

Here the
k=0
cumulant term vanishes because the entries of
E
are
centered. The remainder is written as

\mathtt{R}_{3}:=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\mathtt{R}_{3}^{ij}

(C.17)

where

\mathtt{R}_{3}^{ij}:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[E_{ij}f_{ij}(E)\bigr]-\sum_{k=1}^{2}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{C}_{k+1}(E_{ij})\mathbb{E}\bigl[\partial_{ij}^{\,k}f_{ij}(E)\bigr].

With these notations, we have

\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}]=\left(X_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LG\right)_{vw}Q\right]\right)+X_{2}+\mathtt{R}_{3}.

(C.18)

The next lemma bounds each term on the right-hand side of (
C.18
).

L
EMMA
C.3
.

(i)

For
X_{1}
,

\left|X_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LG\right)_{vw}Q\right]\right|\leq\frac{2}{3}\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-1}\right]}{|z|}+36p\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}]}{|z|^{2}}.

(ii)

For
X_{2}
,

|X_{2}|\leq\frac{4}{9}\beta K\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-1}\right]}{|z|}+178p\beta K\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}]}{|z|^{2}}+700p^{2}\beta K\sigma_{\max}^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-3}]}{|z|^{3}}.

(iii)

For
\mathtt{R}_{3}
, recalling
\mathtt{L}_{n}
from (
C.6
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathtt{R}_{3}|\leq

\displaystyle CK^{2}\beta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\right]

\displaystyle+C^{p}p^{3}\exp\big(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n\big)\frac{(\beta K\sigma_{\max})^{2}}{|z|^{2}}

for an absolute constant
C\geq 1
.

Again, we emphasize that in Lemma
C.3
, we suppress
\chi(E)
from the notation. More precisely, every expectation in the bounds for
X_{1}
,
X_{2}
, and

\mathtt{R}_{3}
is understood to include the factor
\chi(E)
, except for
the explicitly separated cutoff-derivative terms.

With this lemma, the conclusion of Lemma
C.2
follows from (
C.18
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p}\chi(E)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\left|X_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LG\right)_{vw}Q\right]\right|+|X_{2}|+|\mathtt{R}_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq CK\beta\sum_{m=1}^{3}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m}}{|z|^{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-m}\chi(E)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+CK^{2}\beta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\chi(E)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C^{p}p^{3}\exp\big(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n\big)\frac{(\beta K\sigma_{\max})^{2}}{|z|^{2}}.

In the remainder of this section, we prove Lemma
C.3
.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
(i).

From the definition of
L=\mathrm{diag}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{kj}^{2}G_{jj})_{1\leq k\leq n}
in (
C.4
), we have

\mathbb{E}[(LG)_{vw}Q]=\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i,j}v_{i}L_{ij}G_{jw}Q]=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}G_{iw}Q].

(C.19)

From (
C.16
), by product rule, we get

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\mathcal{C}_{2}(E_{ij})\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ij}f_{ij}(E)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ij}(G_{jw}Q)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[(\partial_{ij}G_{jw})Q\right]+\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}Q)\right].

Plugging in
\partial_{ij}G_{jw}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\bigl(G_{ji}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw}\bigr)
by (
C.14
), we further obtain

\displaystyle X_{1}=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{jj}G_{iw}Q\right]+\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{ji}G_{jw}Q\right]+\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}Q)\right].

Comparing with (
C.19
), we arrive at

\displaystyle X_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(LG\right)_{vw}Q\right]=T^{(1)}+T^{(2)},

(C.20)

where

\displaystyle T^{(1)}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{ji}G_{jw}Q\Big],\qquad T^{(2)}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}Q)\Big].

We now provide upper bounds for
T^{(1)}
and
T^{(2)}
.

Step 1.

Estimates for
T^{(1)}
.
Applying Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{ji}G_{jw}\Big|\leq\Big(\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{ij}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

Note that

\sum_{i\neq j}v_{i}^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{ij}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i}v_{i}^{2}\big(\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{ij}|^{2}\big)\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{i}v_{i}^{2}\|Ge_{i}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{2}

and

\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}|^{2}\leq\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\big(\sum_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\big)\leq R_{\max}\|Gw\|^{2}\leq R_{\max}\|G\|^{2}.

We get

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{ji}G_{jw}\Big|\leq\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\,\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}}{|z|^{2}}\leq\frac{2}{3}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}.

by applying (
5.10
) and the assumption
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
.
Consequently,

|T^{(1)}|\leq\frac{2}{3}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-1}\right].

Step 2.

Estimates for
T^{(2)}
.
Recall that

T^{(2)}:=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{jw}\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-1}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p})\right].

For
p\geq 2
, by the product rule,

\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-1}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p})=(p-1)(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-2}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p}+p(\partial_{ij}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw})|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2(p-1)}.

For
p=1
, the first term is absent, and the same final bound follows. Hence, it suffices to bound the two quantities

\mathtt{A}:=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}),\qquad\bar{\mathtt{A}}:=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}).

We first estimate
\mathtt{A}.
From the identity
(zI-E)G=I
, we have
EG=zG-I
. Plugging into
\mathcal{D}=(E-L)G=EG-LG
yields

\mathcal{D}=(zI-L)G-I.

Thus
\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}=(zI-L)\partial_{ij}G-(\partial_{ij}L)G
. Recall
\Delta_{ij}
from (
C.1
). We also have

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}=v^{\mathrm{T}}(zI-L)G\Delta^{ij}Gw-v^{\mathrm{T}}(\partial_{ij}L)Gw.

Denote
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{T}}:=v^{\mathrm{T}}(zI-L)
.
From the definition of
L
in (
C.4
) and the partial derivative formula (
C.14
), we compute

\partial_{ij}l_{s}=\sum_{k}\sigma_{sk}^{2}\partial_{ij}G_{kk}=2(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\sum_{k}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}.

Hence,

v^{\mathrm{T}}(\partial_{ij}L)Gw=2(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{s,k}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\Big(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw}-2\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}\Big).

(C.21)

We are ready to bound

\displaystyle|\mathtt{A}|=\Big|\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})\Big|\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{2}G_{\widetilde{v}i}\Big|+\Big|\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw}\Big|

\displaystyle+2\Big|\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{s,k}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}\Big|

\displaystyle:=T_{1}^{(2)}+T_{2}^{(2)}+T_{3}^{(2)}.

Note that

\|L\|\leq R_{\max}\|G\|\leq 2\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|}\leq\frac{1}{18}|z|

by (
5.10
) and our supposition
|z|>6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. Thus

\|\widetilde{v}\|\leq\|zI-L\|\leq|z|+\|L\|\leq\frac{19}{18}|z|
. We will repeatedly use
\sum_{i}|G_{iw}|^{2}=\|Gw\|^{2}\leq\|G\|^{2}.

Now we estimate
T_{1}^{(2)},T_{2}^{(2)}
and
T_{3}^{(2)}
respectively. For
T_{1}^{(2)}
, since
\max_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
,

\displaystyle T_{1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{i}|v_{i}||G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\cdot\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|Gw\|^{2}\sqrt{\sum_{i}|v_{i}|^{2}\sum_{i}|G_{\widetilde{v}i}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{2}\|\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G\|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\|\widetilde{v}\|\leq\frac{76\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle T_{2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{i}|v_{i}||G_{iw}|\cdot\sum_{j}|G_{jw}||G_{\widetilde{v}j}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sqrt{\sum_{i}|v_{i}|^{2}\sum_{i}|G_{iw}|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\sum_{j}|G_{\widetilde{v}j}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\|\widetilde{v}\|\leq\frac{76\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}.

For
T_{3}^{(2)}
, observe that

\sum_{s}|v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}||G_{sw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{s}|v_{s}||G_{sw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|

and

\sum_{k}|G_{ki}G_{kj}|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k}|G_{ki}|^{2}\sum_{k}|G_{kj}|^{2}}\leq\|G\|^{2}

by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. We further have

\displaystyle T_{3}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}|\cdot|\sigma_{ij}G_{jw}|\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\sqrt{R_{\max}\|Gw\|^{2}}\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G\|^{4}\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

|\mathtt{A}|\leq\frac{76\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}+\frac{76\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}+\frac{8\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}=\frac{160\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{9|z|^{2}}\leq\frac{18\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{2}}.

The same estimate holds for
\bar{\mathtt{A}}
. Indeed, since
\partial_{ij}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}=\overline{\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}},
the preceding argument applies with
G,L,z
replaced by

\overline{G},\overline{L},\overline{z}.
Hence,

|\bar{\mathtt{A}}|\leq\frac{18\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{2}}.

Finally, we bound

\displaystyle|T^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq(p-1)|\mathbb{E}\big[|\mathtt{A}|\cdot\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-2}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p}\big]|+p|\mathbb{E}\big[|\bar{\mathtt{A}}|\cdot|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2(p-1)}\big]|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{36p\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}].

(C.22)

This completes the proof of Lemma
C.3
(i).

Proof of Lemma
C.3
(ii).

For brevity, we suppress the harmless distinction between
\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{p-1}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}^{p}
and
\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1}
. The argument with complex conjugates is identical, using
\partial_{ij}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{vw}=\overline{\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}}.

Recall that

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{C}_{3}(E_{ij})\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\partial_{ij}^{\,2}\!\left(G_{jw}\,\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1}\right)\right].

(C.23)

Using the Leibniz rule and our assumption
|\mathcal{C}_{3}(E_{ij})|=|\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}^{3})|\leq\beta K\sigma_{\max}\sigma_{ij}^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle|X_{2}|\leq\beta K\sigma_{\max}\sum_{s=0}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2-s}(G_{jw})|\cdot|\partial_{ij}^{s}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big].

(C.24)

We estimate the three values
s=0,1,2
separately.

\bullet

Case 1:
s=0
.
We estimate

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}(G_{jw})|.

Using

\partial_{ij}^{2}G=2!G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{2},

one obtains

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}(G_{jw})|\leq 2\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{2}w|=2\sum_{j}\big(\sum_{i}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\big)\cdot|e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{2}w|.

Let
\mathtt{v}=(|v_{i}|)
and
\mathtt{v}
is still a unit vector. By Cauchy-Schwartz, together with
\|\Delta^{ij}\|\leq 1
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}(G_{jw})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\sum_{j}\big(\sum_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathtt{v}_{i}\big)^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{j}|e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{2}w|^{2}}

\displaystyle=2\|\Sigma\mathtt{v}\|\cdot\|G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{2}w\|\leq 2\|\Sigma\|\cdot\|G\|^{3}.

Since
\|\Sigma\|\leq\sqrt{\|\Sigma\|_{1}\cdot\|\Sigma\|_{\infty}}=R_{\max}
,
\|G\|\leq 2/|z|
and
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, this gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}(G_{jw})|\leq 16\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}\leq\frac{4}{9}\frac{1}{|z|}.

Therefore, the contribution from the
s=0
term in (
C.24
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{4}{9}\beta K\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-1}}{|z|}.

\bullet

Case 2:
s=1
.
We estimate

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}|\cdot|\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big].

From (
C.14
), we first have

\partial_{ij}G_{jw}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw}).

By the chain rule,

|\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\leq 2p|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|.

Recall from (
C.21
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\Big(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw}-\mathcal{K}\Big),

where for brevity we define

\mathcal{K}:=2\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}.

We also recall that
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{T}}:=v^{\mathrm{T}}(zI-L)
and
\|\widetilde{v}\|\leq\frac{19}{18}|z|
.

For vectors
\mathsf{v},\mathsf{w}
, we will repeatedly use the following estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|G_{i\mathsf{w}}|^{2}=\|G\mathsf{w}\|^{2}\leq\|G\|^{2}\|\mathsf{w}\|^{2}

(C.25)

Consequently, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{i}|\mathsf{v}_{i}G_{i\mathsf{w}}|\leq\|G\|\|\mathsf{v}\|\|\mathsf{w}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j}|G_{\mathsf{v}j}G_{j\mathsf{w}}|\leq\|G\mathsf{v}\|\|G\mathsf{w}\|\leq\|G\|^{2}\|\mathsf{v}\|\|\mathsf{w}\|.

(C.26)

It follows that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}|\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\big|\sum_{s}v_{s}G_{sw}\big|\cdot\big|\sum_{k}G_{ik}G_{kj}\big|\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}.

(C.27)

Now we are ready to estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(\partial_{ij}G_{jw})(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})\big|\leq

\displaystyle\,\sum_{i,j}\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw})(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw})\mathcal{K}|.

(C.28)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.1
), expanding the product yields four terms. We bound each respectively using (
C.25
) and (
C.26
):

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{ij}G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{ij}G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{iw}|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}||G_{\widetilde{v}j}|\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{jj}G_{iw}G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}\|G\|^{2}\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\sum_{j}|\sigma_{ij}G_{jw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{jj}G_{iw}G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw}|\leq\sigma_{\max}\|G\|^{2}\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{iw}|\sum_{j}|\sigma_{ij}G_{\widetilde{v}j}|\leq\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

Summing these estimates and using the fact that
\sigma_{\max}^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, we obtain

\sum_{i,j}\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw})(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})\big|\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
C.1
), note that by (
C.27
)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw})\mathcal{K}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3}\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|(|G_{ij}G_{jw}|+|G_{jj}G_{iw}|)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G\|^{5},

where in the last inequality, we apply (
C.26
) to bound

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{ij}||G_{jw}|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}v_{i}^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{ij}|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}|^{2}}\leq\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G\|^{2}

and

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{jj}G_{iw}|\leq\|G\|\sum_{i}|v_{i}||G_{iw}|\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\leq R_{\max}\|G\|^{2}.

Combining the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(\partial_{ij}G_{jw})(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|+4\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G\|^{5}.

Using (
5.10
) and our assumption
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, we further have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(\partial_{ij}G_{jw})(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})\big|\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\frac{|z|}{6}\frac{2^{4}}{|z|^{4}}\frac{19|z|}{18}+4\sigma_{\max}\frac{|z|^{2}}{36}\frac{2^{5}}{|z|^{5}}\leq\frac{64\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}.

Consequently, the contribution from the
s=1
term in (
C.24
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\beta K\sigma_{\max}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}|\cdot|\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big]\leq 128p\beta K\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}}{|z|^{2}}.

\bullet

Case 3:
s=2
.
We estimate

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big].

The second derivative gives

|\partial_{ij}^{2}\left(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1}\right)|\leq 2p|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+4p^{2}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-3}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2p\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}||\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}\Big]+4p^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-3}|\Big].

(C.29)

We first estimate

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|.

From (
C.21
), we have

\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\Big[\partial_{ij}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw})+\partial_{ij}(G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})-\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}\Big]

and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw})|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw})|+\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}(G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|.

(C.30)

The first two terms on the right-hand side of (
C.1
) can be estimated similarly. We provide the calculation for the first term. By (
C.14
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\Big[\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{2}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{ij}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{ii})\big|+\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}i}(G_{ij}G_{jw}+G_{jj}G_{iw})\big|\Big].

Note that each term on the right-hand side is bounded by
(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,)\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|
by applying (
C.25
) and (
C.26
). For instance,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\big|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{2}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{ij}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{ii})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}+\sigma_{\max}\|G\|^{2}\|\widetilde{v}\|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\sum_{i}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,)\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

Continuing from (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw})|\leq 8\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\,\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|+\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|.

It remains to estimate
\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|
, where

\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}=2\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}\partial_{ij}(G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}).

We first estimate
|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|
. Expanding
\partial_{ij}(G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw})
using the product rule and (
C.14
) yields several terms, each of which admits a similar treatment. We present the calculation for one representative term. For instance,

\displaystyle\sum_{s,k}|v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}(\partial_{ij}G_{ki})G_{kj}G_{sw}|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sum_{s,k}|v_{s}(G_{ki}G_{jj}+G_{kj}G_{ij})G_{kj}G_{sw}|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|\sum_{s}|v_{s}G_{sw}|\sum_{k}(|G_{ki}|+|G_{kj}|)|G_{kj}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}

where we applied (
C.25
) and (
C.26
) in the last inequality. Hence,

|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|\leq 12\,\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}

and by Cauchy-Schwarz and (
C.25
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}\partial_{ij}\mathcal{K}|\leq 12\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}|\leq 12\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{4}\cdot R_{\max}\|G\|=12\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G\|^{5}.

Finally, using
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
and
\|G\|\leq 2/|z|
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw})|

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G\|^{4}\|\widetilde{v}\|+12\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G\|^{5}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{\max}\frac{|z|}{{6}}\frac{2^{4}}{|z|^{4}}\frac{19|z|}{18}+12\sigma_{\max}\frac{|z|^{3}}{{6}^{3}}\frac{2^{5}}{|z|^{5}}<25\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}.

(C.31)

Next, we bound

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2}.

Continuing from (
C.21
), we get

\displaystyle(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})^{2}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-2}\Big[(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})^{2}+\mathcal{K}^{2}-2\mathcal{K}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})\Big],

where

\mathcal{K}=2\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{ki}G_{kj}G_{sw}.

Plugging this expansion into
\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})^{2}|
, we first observe from (
C.27
) that

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}||\mathcal{K}|^{2}\leq(2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{3})^{2}\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}||G_{jw}|\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{4}\|G\|^{6}\cdot R_{\max}\|G\|.

Next, using (
C.25
) and (
C.26
), we also have

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})||\mathcal{K}|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 4\sigma_{\max}^{4}\|G\|^{3}\left(\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\cdot|G_{jw}|^{2}+\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{iw}|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}j}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 8\sigma_{\max}^{4}\|G\|^{6}\|\widetilde{v}\|.

Finally, we claim that

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(G_{\widetilde{v}i}G_{jw}+G_{\widetilde{v}j}G_{iw})^{2}|\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}\|^{2}.

To see this, we expand the square in the summation and show that each term is bounded by
\|G\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}\|^{2}
, using a similar calculation with (
C.25
) and (
C.26
). For instance,

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}G_{jw}G_{\widetilde{v}i}^{2}G_{jw}^{2}|\leq\|G\|^{2}\|\widetilde{v}\|\sum_{i}|v_{i}G_{\widetilde{v}i}|\sum_{j}|G_{jw}|^{2}\leq\|G\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}\|^{2},

and the remaining terms are estimated in the same manner.

Hence, together with
\|\widetilde{v}\|\leq\frac{19}{18}|z|
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}(\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw})^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{\max}^{4}R_{\max}\|G\|^{7}+8\sigma_{\max}^{4}\|G\|^{6}\|\widetilde{v}\|+4\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{3}\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{3}}(4\frac{|z|^{4}}{6^{4}}\frac{2^{4}}{|z|^{4}}+8\frac{|z|^{2}}{6^{2}}\frac{2^{3}}{|z|^{3}}\frac{19|z|}{18}+4\frac{2^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\frac{19^{2}|z|^{2}}{18^{2}})<175\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{3}}.

(C.32)

We continue from (
C.1
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big]\leq 50p\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-2}}{|z|^{2}}+700p^{2}\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-3}}{|z|^{3}}.

Thus, the contribution from the
s=2
term in (
C.24
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\beta K\sigma_{\max}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}||\partial_{ij}^{2}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1})|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 50p\beta K\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}|D_{vw}|^{2p-2}}{|z|^{2}}+700p^{2}\beta K\sigma_{\max}^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|^{2p-3}}{|z|^{3}}.

Combining all the above estimates completes the proof of Lemma
C.3
(ii).

A third-order bound for the remainder term.

Before turning to the remainder term, we record an additional derivative estimate required by the cumulant remainder formula in Lemma
C.1
. This estimate serves as a third-order analogue of those used in bounding
X_{2}
above, corresponding to the third-order derivatives of

G_{jw}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{2p-1}.

The argument follows the structure of the
s=0,1,2
cases in the proof of Lemma
C.3
(ii). We isolate this estimate here for use in the remainder analysis.

Recall from the truncation parameter
\mathtt{L}_{n}
from (
C.6
). For
1\leq i,j\leq n
and
|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}
, let

E^{ij(x)}:=E+(x-E_{ij})\Delta^{ij},\qquad G^{ij(x)}(z):=(zI-E^{ij(x)})^{-1}.

Define

L^{ij(x)}:=\operatorname{diag}(l_{1}^{ij(x)},\dots,l_{n}^{ij(x)})\qquad\text{with}\quad l_{s}^{ij(x)}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{kk}^{ij(x)},

and

\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}:=(E^{ij(x)}-L^{ij(x)})G^{ij(x)}(z).

We use the notation
\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}=v^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}w
and
G_{vw}^{ij(x)}=v^{\mathrm{T}}G^{ij(x)}w
. Set

\displaystyle f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)}):=G_{jw}^{ij(x)}\left(\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}\right)^{2p-1}.

(C.33)

Recall from (
C.7
) the event

\Omega_{n}:=\left\{\|E\|\leq 3.5\sqrt{R_{\max}},\quad\max_{a,b}|E_{ab}|\leq 2\mathtt{L}_{n}\right\}.

The goal is to prove the following estimate on the event
\Omega_{n}
(the support of
\chi(E)
):

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}\left|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})\right|\leq C\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}

(C.34)

for an absolute constant
C>0
.

We start with some preliminary estimates. On the event
\Omega_{n}
, we have, for
|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\|E^{ij(x)}\|\leq\|E\|+(|x|+|E_{ij}|)\|\Delta_{ij}\|\leq 3.5\sqrt{R_{\max}}+3\mathtt{L}_{n}\leq 4\sqrt{R_{\max}}

(C.35)

provided the constant
C_{0}
in Assumption
1.2
is chosen sufficiently large. Hence, for
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
, by the Neumann expansion, we have

\displaystyle\|G^{ij(x)}(z)\|\leq\frac{3}{|z|}.

(C.36)

Next, we show that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}-\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\leq 22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}.

(C.37)

To see this, for
|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}
, we bound

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}-\mathcal{D}_{vw}|=|v^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}-\mathcal{D})w|\leq\|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}-\mathcal{D}\|.

From the definitions of
\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}
and
\mathcal{D}
, we further have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}-\mathcal{D}_{vw}|

\displaystyle\leq\|(E^{ij(x)}-L^{ij(x)})G^{ij(x)}(z)-(E-L)G(z)\|

\displaystyle=\|[(E^{ij(x)}-E)-(L^{ij(x)}-L)]G^{ij(x)}(z)+(E-L)(G^{ij(x)}(z)-G(z))\|

\displaystyle\leq(\|E^{ij(x)}-E\|+\|L^{ij(x)}-L\|)\|G^{ij(x)}(z)\|+(\|E\|+\|L\|)\|G^{ij(x)}(z)-G(z)\|.

For simplicity, denote
P^{ij}:=E^{ij(x)}-E=(x-E_{ij})\Delta^{ij}
. Similar to (
C.35
), we have

\|E^{ij(x)}-E\|=\|P^{ij}\|\leq(|x|+|E_{ij}|)\|\Delta_{ij}\|\leq 3\mathtt{L}_{n}.

Next, by the resolvent identity, we have

\displaystyle G^{ij(x)}(z)-G(z)=G^{ij(x)}(z)P^{ij}G(z)

Thus, by (
C.36
) and (
5.10
),

\displaystyle\|G^{ij(x)}(z)-G(z)\|=\|G^{ij(x)}(z)\|\cdot\|P^{ij}\|\cdot\|G(z)\|\leq 18\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|^{2}}.

(C.38)

Then

\displaystyle\|L^{ij(x)}-L\|=\max_{s}|l_{s}^{ij(x)}-l_{s}|

\displaystyle=\max_{s}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{sk}^{2}|G_{kk}^{ij(x)}(z)-G_{kk}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}(z)-G(z)\|\leq 18\mathtt{L}_{n}\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{L}_{n}

by the assumption
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\|L\|=\max_{s}|l_{s}|\leq\max_{s}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{sk}^{2}|G_{kk}(z)|\leq R_{\max}\|G(z)\|\leq 2\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|}.

It follows that

\|E\|+\|L\|\leq 3.5\sqrt{R_{\max}}+2\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|}\leq\left(\frac{7}{12}+\frac{1}{18}\right)|z|.

Combining the above estimates, we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}-\mathcal{D}_{vw}|\leq 10.5\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}+18\left(\frac{7}{12}+\frac{1}{18}\right)\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}=22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}.

Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}|\mathcal{D}^{ij}_{vw}(x)|\leq|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}.

(C.39)

Now, we proceed to the proof of (
C.34
). By the Leibniz rule,

\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})=\sum_{s=0}^{3}\binom{3}{s}\left(\partial_{ij}^{3-s}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}\right)\left(\partial_{ij}^{s}(\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw})^{2p-1}\right).

We estimate the four cases
s=0,1,2,3
separately. The cases
s=0,1,2
follow similar arguments to those used for
X_{2}
in (
C.24
).

First, for
s=0
, using

\partial_{ij}^{3}G^{ij(x)}=3!G^{ij(x)}(\Delta^{ij}G^{ij(x)})^{3}

and the same estimate used for the
s=0
term in the
X_{2}
bound, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{3}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|\leq 6R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{4}\leq 6R_{\max}\frac{3^{4}}{|z|^{4}}\leq\frac{27}{2|z|^{2}}.

Therefore,

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{3}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|\cdot|(\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw})^{2p-1}|\leq\frac{27}{2|z|^{2}}|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2p-1}.

Next, for
s=1
, since

|\partial_{ij}(\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw})^{2p-1}|\leq 2p|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2p-2}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|,

we aim to bound

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{ij}(x)||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|.

This is proved exactly as the
s=1
estimate for
X_{2}
, but with an extra factor of
G
. We sketch the key steps here. Expand

\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}=(1+\delta_{ij})^{-1}\left(G_{\widetilde{v}_{x}i}^{ij(x)}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}+G_{\widetilde{v}_{x}j}^{ij(x)}G_{iw}^{ij(x)}-\mathcal{K}_{x}\right),

where
\widetilde{v}_{x}^{\mathrm{T}}:=v^{\mathrm{T}}(zI-L^{ij(x)})
and

\mathcal{K}_{x}:=2\sum_{s,k}v_{s}\sigma_{sk}^{2}G_{ki}^{ij(x)}G_{kj}^{ij(x)}G_{sw}^{ij(x)}.

Since

\|L^{ij(x)}\|\leq\max_{s}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sigma_{sk}^{2}|G^{ij(x)}(z)|\leq R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}(z)\|\leq 3\frac{R_{\max}}{|z|}\leq\frac{1}{12}|z|,

we have
\|\widetilde{v}_{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\|\leq\frac{13}{12}|z|.
Similar to (
C.27
), we obtain

|\mathcal{K}_{x}|\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{3}.

For the second derivative of the resolvent

\displaystyle\partial_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}=2(1+\delta_{ij})^{-2}\Big[\big((G^{ij(x)}_{ij})^{2}+G^{ij(x)}_{ii}G^{ij(x)}_{jj}\big)G^{ij(x)}_{jw}+2G^{ij(x)}_{ij}G^{ij(x)}_{jj}G^{ij(x)}_{iw}\Big],

we apply the crude upper bound

\displaystyle|\partial_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|\leq 4\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{2}\big(|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|+|G_{iw}^{ij(x)}|\big).

Multiplying this by the expansion of
\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}
and applying the identical Cauchy-Schwarz estimates used for
X_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\leq 16\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}_{x}\|+16\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{6}

\displaystyle\leq 16\sigma_{\max}\left(\frac{|z|}{6}\right)\frac{3^{5}}{|z|^{5}}\left(\frac{13|z|}{12}\right)+16\sigma_{\max}\left(\frac{|z|^{3}}{216}\right)\frac{3^{6}}{|z|^{6}}

\displaystyle\leq 702\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}+54\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}=756\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}.

Hence the
s=1
contribution is bounded by

\displaystyle 1512p\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2p-2}.

For
s=2
, we use

|\partial_{ij}^{2}\left((\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw})^{2p-1}\right)|\leq 2p|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2p-2}|\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|+4p^{2}|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2p-3}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}_{vw}|^{2}.

The first estimate needed is

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij}(x)||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij}(x)|.

Using
|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|\leq 2\|G^{ij(x)}\|(|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|+|G_{iw}^{ij(x)}|)
and multiplying by the bounds established in (
C.1
), we pull out an extra factor of
2\|G^{ij(x)}\|
and double the sum to account for
|G_{iw}|
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\leq 32\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{R_{\max}}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{5}\|\widetilde{v}_{x}\|+48\sigma_{\max}^{2}R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{6}

\displaystyle\leq 1404\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}+162\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}=1566\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}.

We also require the estimate for

\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij}(x)||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2}.

Again, using
|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|\leq 2\|G^{ij(x)}\|(|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|+|G_{iw}^{ij(x)}|)
to the estimates from (
C.1
) yields:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|\partial_{ij}G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 24\sigma_{\max}^{2}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{6}\|\widetilde{v}_{x}\|^{2}+16\sigma_{\max}^{4}R_{\max}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{8}

\displaystyle\leq 24\sigma_{\max}^{2}\frac{3^{6}}{|z|^{6}}\left(\frac{169|z|^{2}}{144}\right)+16\sigma_{\max}^{2}\left(\frac{|z|^{4}}{1296}\right)\frac{3^{8}}{|z|^{8}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{4}}.

Therefore, the total
s=2
contribution is bounded by

Cp\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-2}+Cp^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{4}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-3}.

Finally, for
s=3
, applying the generalized chain rule (Faà di Bruno’s formula
[
45
, p. 137]
) to the third derivative of
(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)})^{2p-1}
yields:

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{ij}^{3}\Big((\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)})^{2p-1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2p|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-2}|\partial_{ij}^{3}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\quad+12p^{2}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-3}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\quad+8p^{3}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-4}|\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{3}.

Bounding this requires three separate estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}^{3}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}},

(C.40)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{4}},

(C.41)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}||\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{3}}{|z|^{5}},

(C.42)

where
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
are absolute constants (which are all bounded
10^{5}
).

We briefly sketch the proof of these estimates. For (
C.40
), from the identity
\mathcal{D}=(zI-L)G-I
, we have

\partial_{ij}^{3}\mathcal{D}=(zI-L)\partial_{ij}^{3}G-3(\partial_{ij}L)\partial_{ij}^{2}G-3(\partial_{ij}^{2}L)\partial_{ij}G-(\partial_{ij}^{3}L)G.

Then we multiply this expansion with
|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|
and sum over
i,j
. The first term is bounded by expanding
\partial_{ij}^{3}G=3!G(\Delta^{ij}G)^{3}
. The factor
|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|
combined with the variance weight generates one factor of
\sigma_{\max}
, while the remaining Green functions are controlled by (
C.25
) and (
C.26
). This yields the
C_{1}\sigma_{\max}|z|^{-3}
bound.
The terms containing
\partial_{ij}^{a}L
are handled by expanding

\partial_{ij}^{a}l_{s}=\sum_{k}\sigma_{sk}^{2}\partial_{ij}^{a}G_{kk}^{ij(x)},

together with

|\partial_{ij}^{a}G_{kk}^{ij(x)}|\leq\widetilde{C}_{a}\|G^{ij(x)}\|^{a-1}\sum_{s\in\{i,j\}}|G_{ks}^{ij(x)}|^{2}.

and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. This proves (
C.40
).

The estimates (
C.41
) and (
C.42
) follow by
combining the already proved bounds for
\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}
and
\partial_{ij}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}
, and the factor
|G_{jw}^{ij(x)}|
. Note that each additional
\partial_{ij}\mathcal{D}
contributes one factor

\sigma_{\max}/|z|
in the estimate, exactly as in the
s=2
case of the
X_{2}
estimate.

Consequently, the
s=3
contribution is bounded by

2p\,C_{1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{|z|^{3}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-2}+12p^{2}\,C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{|z|^{4}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-3}+8p^{3}\,C_{3}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{3}}{|z|^{5}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-4}.

Combining the four cases
s=0,1,2,3
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}\left|\partial_{ij}^{3}\left(G_{jw}^{ij(x)}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)})^{2p-1}\right)\right|\leq C\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-m}.

(C.43)

Using
|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|\leq|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\mathtt{L}_{n}/|z|
from (
C.39
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}\left|\partial_{ij}^{3}\left(G_{jw}^{ij(x)}(\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)})^{2p-1}\right)\right|\leq C\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}.

This proves (
C.34
).

Proof of Lemma
C.3
(iii).

We estimate the remainder term in the cumulant expansion with
l=2
. Recall from (
C.17
) that

\mathtt{R}_{3}=\sum_{i,j}v_{i}\mathtt{R}_{3}^{ij}.

By Lemma
C.1
,

\displaystyle|\mathtt{R}_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}\mathtt{R}_{3}^{ij}|

\displaystyle\leq 16\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{4}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq t}|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij}+x\Delta^{ij})|\right]\quad

\displaystyle(\text{Term 1})

\displaystyle+16\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{8}\mathbf{1}_{|E_{ij}|>t}\right]\right)^{1/2}\quad

\displaystyle(\text{Term 2})

We choose
t=\mathtt{L}_{n}
. In the general sub-Gaussian regime,
\mathtt{L}_{n}=5\sqrt{D+6}K\sigma_{\max}\log n,
, while in the bounded sparse-entry regime, it is equal to zero. Here, as before, the cutoff
\chi
is suppressed from the notation. Also, as explained in the beginning of Section
C.1
, terms with derivatives applied to
\chi
are negligible.

Let us consider the general sub-Gaussian regime. We first estimate Term 1. Set

\theta:=\beta K\sigma_{\max}.

By Assumption
1.1
,

\mathbb{E}|E_{ij}|^{4}\leq\beta^{2}K^{2}\sigma_{\max}^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}=\theta^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}.

Hence, by (
C.34
), the contribution from Term 1 is bounded by

\displaystyle 16\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{4}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij}+x\Delta^{ij})|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 16\theta^{2}\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}}\left|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\theta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\right].

It remains to estimate Term 2. By the sub-Gaussian tail assumption, we have

\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{8}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|>\mathtt{L}_{n}\}}\right]\right)^{1/2}\leq\theta^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\exp(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n).

(C.44)

To see this, note that the case
\sigma_{ij}=0
is trivial. For
\sigma_{ij}>0
, write
E_{ij}=\sigma_{ij}\xi_{ij}
with
\|\xi_{ij}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K
. A standard sub-Gaussian tail integration yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{8}\mathbf{1}_{\{|E_{ij}|>\mathtt{L}_{n}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(K\sigma_{ij})^{8}\left(1+\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{K\sigma_{ij}}\right)^{8}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}^{2}}{K^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(K\sigma_{ij}+\mathtt{L}_{n}\right)^{8}\exp\left(-\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}^{2}}{K^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}}\right).

(C.45)

Because
\sigma_{ij}\leq\sigma_{\max}
and
\mathtt{L}_{n}=5\sqrt{D+6}K\sigma_{\max}\log n
, we bound

\left(K\sigma_{ij}+\mathtt{L}_{n}\right)^{8}\leq CD^{4}K^{8}\sigma_{\max}^{8}(\log n)^{8}.

Moreover, we write

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}^{2}}{K^{2}\sigma_{ij}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{ij}^{4}}{\sigma_{\max}^{4}}\left[\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{4}}{\sigma_{ij}^{4}}\exp\left(-25(D+6)\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sigma_{ij}^{2}}\log^{2}n\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{ij}^{4}}{\sigma_{\max}^{4}}\exp\big(-25(D+6)\log^{2}n\big),

where we used the fact that the function
y\mapsto y^{2}\exp(-cy)
is strictly decreasing for
y\geq 1
and large
c>0
, and then
y^{2}\exp(-cy)\leq\exp(-c)
.

Thus, the right-hand side of (
C.1
) is bounded by
CD^{4}K^{8}\sigma_{\max}^{4}\sigma_{ij}^{4}(\log n)^{8}\exp(-25(D+6)\log^{2}n).

To get the desired bound (
C.44
), we only need this quantity

D^{4}K^{8}\sigma_{\max}^{4}\sigma_{ij}^{4}(\log n)^{8}\exp(-25(D+6)\log^{2}n)\leq\theta^{4}\sigma_{ij}^{4}\exp(-20(D+6)\log^{2}n),

where
\theta=\beta K\sigma_{\max}
. Since
\beta\geq 1
, we require

\displaystyle\exp(25(D+6)\log^{2}n)\geq D^{4}K^{4}\exp(20(D+6)\log^{2}n).

(C.46)

We now use Assumption
1.2
. Since
R_{\max}\leq n\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and
\sqrt{R_{\max}}\geq C_{0}(D+8)K\sigma_{\max}\log n,

we have

K\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{C_{0}(D+8)\log n}.

Hence, (
C.46
) holds and (
C.44
) is proved.

Now, Term 2 is bounded by

\displaystyle 16\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{8}\mathbf{1}_{|E_{ij}|>t}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\exp(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n)\theta^{2}\sum_{i,j}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}\left||v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}.

Recall that the cutoff
\chi
is suppressed from the notation. On the support
\Omega_{n}
of
\chi
, (
C.43
) holds. Moreover, by (
C.35
) and (
C.36
), we also have this crude bound

\|\mathcal{D}^{ij(x)}\|=\|(E^{ij(x)}-L^{ij(x)})G^{ij(x)}\|\leq C.

Therefore,

|\mathcal{D}_{vw}^{ij(x)}|^{2p-m}\leq C^{p},\qquad 1\leq m\leq 4.

It follows from (
C.43
) that

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}\left||v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\leq C^{p}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}.

Since
\sigma_{\max}\leq\sqrt{R_{\max}}\leq|z|/6
, we simplify this bound to

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}\left||v_{i}|\sigma_{ij}^{2}\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\leq C^{p}p^{3}\frac{1}{|z|^{2}}.

Combining the above bound and (
C.44
), the total contribution of Term 2 is bounded by

\displaystyle 16\sum_{i,j}|v_{i}|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|x|\leq|E_{ij}|}|\partial_{ij}^{3}f_{ij}(E^{ij(x)})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|E_{ij}|^{8}\mathbf{1}_{|E_{ij}|>t}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C^{p}p^{3}\exp\big(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n\big)\frac{\theta^{2}}{|z|^{2}}.

Finally, we arrive at

\displaystyle|\mathtt{R}_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq C\theta^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\right]+C^{p}p^{3}\exp\big(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n\big)\frac{\theta^{2}}{|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle=C(K\beta)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{4}p^{m-1}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{m+1}}{|z|^{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\mathcal{D}_{vw}|+22\frac{\mathtt{L}_{n}}{|z|}\right)^{2p-m}\right]+C^{p}p^{3}\exp\big(-10(D+6)\log^{2}n\big)\frac{\theta^{2}}{|z|^{2}}.

The bounded sparse-entry regime follows the same approach. Indeed, the Term 2 in the bound for
|\mathtt{R}_{3}|
is equal to zero because

\max_{i,j}|E_{ij}|\leq\mathtt{L}_{n}
almost surely. This simplifies the derivation. We skip the details. The proof of Lemma
C.3
is now complete.

Appendix D
Proof of Proposition
8.2

Recall that

\displaystyle H=\mathrm{diag}(h_{1},\ldots,h_{n}),\qquad h_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j};

\displaystyle L=\operatorname{diag}(l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}),\qquad l_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}G_{jj}.

Set

\mathcal{D}:=(E-L)G,\qquad\mathcal{D}_{ii}:=e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{D}e_{i}.

Note that
\max_{i}|\phi_{i}(z)|\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}
by Lemma
5.1
. Define

d_{i}:=\phi_{i}-G_{ii},\qquad d:=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})^{\mathrm{T}}

We first bound
\|d\|_{\infty}
. From
(zI-E)G=I,

we have
EG=zG-I.

Therefore, taking the
(i,i)
-entry of
\mathcal{D}=(E-L)G
yields

\mathcal{D}_{ii}=(EG)_{ii}-l_{i}G_{ii}=(z-l_{i})G_{ii}-1.

Equivalently,

(z-l_{i})G_{ii}=1+\mathcal{D}_{ii}.

On the other hand, we have from the QVE that
(z-h_{i})\phi_{i}=1.

Thus

\displaystyle(z-h_{i})(\phi_{i}-G_{ii})

\displaystyle=(z-h_{i})\phi_{i}-(z-h_{i})G_{ii}

\displaystyle=1-(z-h_{i})G_{ii}

\displaystyle=(z-l_{i})G_{ii}-\mathcal{D}_{ii}-(z-h_{i})G_{ii}

\displaystyle=(h_{i}-l_{i})G_{ii}-\mathcal{D}_{ii}.

Multiplying by
\phi_{i}
, since
\phi_{i}=(z-h_{i})^{-1}
, yields

d_{i}=\phi_{i}G_{ii}(h_{i}-l_{i})-\phi_{i}\mathcal{D}_{ii}.

(D.1)

Moreover,

h_{i}-l_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(\phi_{j}-G_{jj})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}d_{j}.

Substituting this into (
D.1
), we obtain

d_{i}=\phi_{i}G_{ii}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\Big)d_{j}-\phi_{i}\mathcal{D}_{ii}.

Thus, by taking the
\ell_{\infty}
norm, we get

\|d\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{i}|\phi_{i}G_{ii}|\cdot R_{\max}\|d\|_{\infty}+\max_{i}|\phi_{i}|\cdot\max_{i}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|.

Since

\max_{i}|\phi_{i}G_{ii}|\leq\frac{3}{2|z|}\cdot\frac{2}{|z|}=\frac{3}{|z|^{2}},

we bound

\|d\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\|d\|_{\infty}+\frac{3}{2|z|}\max_{i}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|.

Since
|z|^{2}\geq 36R_{\max}
by our assumption, we further obtain

\|d\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{|z|}\max_{i}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|.

(D.2)

We now return to
v^{\mathrm{T}}(L-H)Gw
. Since
v,w
are unit vectors,

|v^{\mathrm{T}}(L-H)Gw|\leq\|L-H\|\cdot\|G\|,

where

\displaystyle\|L-H\|=\max_{i}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(G_{jj}-\phi_{j})\Big|\leq R_{\max}\|d\|_{\infty}.

Combining this with
\|G\|\leq 2/|z|
and
(
D.2
), we arrive at

|v^{\mathrm{T}}(L-H)Gw|\leq\frac{4R_{\max}}{|z|^{2}}\max_{i}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|\leq\frac{1}{9}\max_{i}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|.

(D.3)

To bound
\mathcal{D}_{ii}
, we apply
Proposition
8.1
with
v=w=e_{i}
and take a union bound to obtain

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|\mathcal{D}_{ii}|\leq C\left(\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{|z|}+n^{-500}\right)

with probability at least
1-n^{-D-4}
. In the bounded sparse-entry regime,
the analogous bound follows without the
n^{-500}
term.

Combining the above estimate with (
D.3
) proves Proposition
8.2
.

Appendix E
Proof of Corollary
2

Write

\Xi(z)=G(z)-\Phi(z).

We start with the following consequence of the isotropic local law:

C
OROLLARY
3

(Compressed local law on the signal subspace)
.

Fix
D>0
. Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
4
hold. Then, for every fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}_{+}
with
|z|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}},

with probability at least
1-n^{-D-2}
,

\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))U\right\|\leq Cr\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{|z|^{2}}.

Proof of Corollary
3
.

Since

\displaystyle\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))U\right\|\leq\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))U\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\big|{e_{i}}^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))Ue_{j}\big|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq r\max_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\big|{e_{i}}^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))Ue_{j}\big|,

the conclusion of Corollary
3
follows directly from Theorem
4
and the union bounds.
∎

To extend the pointwise bound Corollary
3
to the spectral domain
\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:6\sqrt{R_{\max}}\leq|z|\leq 2R_{\max}^{3}\}
, we use a standard
\varepsilon
-net argument. Set

\eta:=\min\left\{1,\frac{r\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{20}\right\}.

Let
\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
be a
\eta
-net of
\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
. A simple volume argument (see for instance
[
74
, Lemma 3.3]
) shows that

|\mathcal{N}_{\eta}|\leq\left(1+\frac{8R_{\max}^{3}}{\eta}\right)^{2}.

Since
R_{\max}\leq n^{100}
and
\sigma_{\max}^{-1}\leq n^{100}
, we have
\mathfrak{m}_{D}\geq cn^{-100}
for an absolute constant
c>0
. Thus
R_{\max}^{3}/\eta\leq C^{\prime}n^{400}
and
|\mathcal{N}_{\eta}|\leq Cn^{800}
.

Applying Corollary
3
with
D_{1}=D+810
and taking a union bound over
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}}|z|^{2}\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U\right\|\geq Cr\mathfrak{m}_{D_{1}}\right)\leq|\mathcal{N}_{\eta}|n^{-D-814}<n^{-D-10}.

Using
\mathfrak{m}_{D_{1}}=\mathfrak{m}_{D+810}\leq C^{\prime}\mathfrak{m}_{D}
, we establish that with probability at least
1-n^{-D-10}
,

\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}}|z|^{2}\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U\right\|\leq Cr\mathfrak{m}_{D}.

Now we extend the estimate from
\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
to the whole domain
\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
. Define

f(z):=z^{2}U^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)U,\qquad g(z):=z^{2}U^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U.

We first show that
f
and
g
are Lipschitz on
\mathcal{S}
.

Let
z,w\in\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
and assume without loss of generality that
|z|\geq|w|\geq 6\sqrt{R_{\max}}
. By the resolvent identity,

G(z)-G(w)=(w-z)G(z)G(w).

Using
\|G(z)\|\leq 2/|z|
and
\|G(w)\|\leq 2/|w|
, we get

\displaystyle\|f(z)-f(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq|z||z-w|\|G(z)\|+|w||z-w|\|G(z)\|

\displaystyle\quad+|w|^{2}\|G(z)-G(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2|z-w|+2\frac{|w|}{|z|}|z-w|+4\frac{|w|}{|z|}|z-w|

\displaystyle\leq 8|z-w|.

Hence
f
is
8
-Lipschitz on
\mathcal{S}
.

Next we prove the Lipschitz bound for
g
. For each
i\in[n]
, the QVE gives

\frac{1}{\phi_{i}(z)}=z-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(z),\qquad\frac{1}{\phi_{i}(w)}=w-\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\phi_{j}(w).

Subtracting the two identities and multiplying by
\phi_{i}(z)\phi_{i}(w)
, we obtain

\phi_{i}(z)-\phi_{i}(w)=\phi_{i}(z)\phi_{i}(w)\left[w-z+\sum_{j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(\phi_{j}(z)-\phi_{j}(w))\right].

Therefore, by Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle\|\Phi(z)-\Phi(w)\|\leq\frac{9}{4|z||w|}\left(|z-w|+R_{\max}\|\Phi(z)-\Phi(w)\|\right).

Since
|z||w|\geq 36R_{\max}
, we have

\frac{9R_{\max}}{4|z||w|}\leq\frac{1}{16}.

Thus,

\|\Phi(z)-\Phi(w)\|\leq\frac{12}{5}\frac{|z-w|}{|z||w|}.

Using again Lemma
5.1
, we have
\|\Phi(z)\|\leq 3/(2|z|)
. Hence

\displaystyle\|g(z)-g(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq|z||z-w|\|\Phi(z)\|+|w||z-w|\|\Phi(z)\|

\displaystyle\quad+|w|^{2}\|\Phi(z)-\Phi(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}|z-w|+\frac{3}{2}\frac{|w|}{|z|}|z-w|+\frac{12}{5}\frac{|w|}{|z|}|z-w|

\displaystyle<6|z-w|.

Therefore
g
is
6
-Lipschitz on
\mathcal{S}
.

Now take any
z\in\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
. By the definition of the
\eta
-net, there exists
w\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
such that
|z-w|\leq\eta
. Then

\displaystyle|z|^{2}\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U\right\|

\displaystyle=\|f(z)-g(z)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|f(z)-f(w)\|+\|f(w)-g(w)\|+\|g(w)-g(z)\|

\displaystyle\leq 14\eta+|w|^{2}\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(w)U\right\|.

Since
\eta\leq\frac{r\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{20}
, we get

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}}|z|^{2}\left\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U\right\|\leq\frac{14r\mathfrak{m}_{D}}{20}+Cr\mathfrak{m}_{D}\leq C^{\prime}r\mathfrak{m}_{D}

with probability at least
1-n^{-D-10}
. This completes the proof of (
6.3
).

The proof of (
6.4
) is identical to the proof
of (
6.3
). One applies Theorem
4
to the pairs
(e_{i},u_{a})
for
i\in[n]
and
a\in[r]
, takes a union bound
over
i,a
, and uses the same net argument over
\mathcal{S}_{\rm out}
.

Finally,

e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Q\Xi(z)U=e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U-e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}U\cdot U^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(z)U,

so (
6.5
) follows from
(
6.4
) and
(
6.3
).

Appendix F
Proofs of (
2.7
) and (
2.8
)

We provide the proofs of (
2.7
) and (
2.8
) below.

Proof of (
2.7
):
First, by
[
25
, Exercises VII. 1. 9– 1.11]
,

\|\sin\angle(U_{l:s},\widetilde{U}_{l:s})\|=\|(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|=\|P_{U_{l:s}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|.

From the decomposition
P_{U_{l:s}}=P_{U_{s}}-P_{U_{l-1}}
, we can express

P_{U_{l:s}^{\perp}}=I-P_{U_{l:s}}=(I-P_{U_{s}})+P_{U_{l-1}}=P_{U_{s}^{\perp}}+P_{U_{l-1}}.

It follows that

\|P_{U_{l:s}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|\leq\|P_{U_{s}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|+\|P_{U_{l-1}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|.

(F.1)

For the first term, observe that the columns of
\widetilde{U}_{l:s}
are a subset of the columns of
\widetilde{U}_{s}
. Thus

\|P_{U_{s}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|\leq\|P_{U_{s}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{s}\|=\|\sin\angle(U_{s},\widetilde{U}_{s})\|.

For the second term in (
F.1
), since
\widetilde{U}_{l:s}
is orthogonal to
\widetilde{U}_{l-1}
, we have
P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}=\widetilde{U}_{l:s}
. Then

P_{U_{l-1}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}=P_{U_{l-1}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s},

and therefore,

\|P_{U_{l-1}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|\leq\|P_{U_{l-1}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|=\|\sin\angle(U_{l-1},\widetilde{U}_{l-1})\|.

Substituting these two bounds back into (
F.1
) completes the proof of (
2.7
).

Proof of (
2.8
):
Let
U_{l:s}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}=WDV^{\mathrm{T}}
be the singular value decomposition. We choose
O=WV^{\mathrm{T}}
and then decompose

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{l:s}-U_{l:s}O

\displaystyle=(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}+P_{U_{l:s}}(\widetilde{U}_{l:s}-U_{l:s}O)

\displaystyle=(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}+U_{l:s}(U_{l:s}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}-O).

Taking the
2\to\infty
norm and applying the triangle inequality yields

\|\widetilde{U}_{l:s}-U_{l:s}O\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}+\|U_{l:s}(U_{l:s}^{T}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}-O)\|_{2,\infty}.

(F.2)

By our choice of
O
, the second term in (
F.2
) is bounded by

\|U_{l:s}W(D-I)V^{\mathrm{T}}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|U_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}\|W(I-D)V^{\mathrm{T}}\|=\|U_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}\|I-D\|.

Note that the diagonal entries of
D
are the cosines of the principal angles
\theta_{i}
. The entries of
I-D^{2}
are exactly
\sin^{2}\theta_{i}
. Because
D\succeq 0
, we have
I-D\preceq I-D^{2}
, which implies

\|I-D\|\leq\|I-D^{2}\|=\|\sin\angle(U_{l:s},\widetilde{U}_{l:s})\|^{2}.

This proves the main inequality in (
2.8
). It remains to bound
\|(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}
. Using
I-P_{U_{l:s}}=(I-P_{U_{s}})+P_{U_{l-1}}
, we expand

(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}=(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}+P_{U_{l-1}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}.

Thus

\|(I-P_{U_{l:s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}+\|P_{U_{l-1}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}.

(F.3)

For the first term, since
(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}
consists of a subset of the columns of
(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{s}
, we get

\|(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(I-P_{U_{s}})\widetilde{U}_{s}\|_{2,\infty}.

For the second term, plugging in
P_{U_{l-1}}=U_{l-1}U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}
, we find

\|P_{U_{l-1}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|_{2,\infty}=\|U_{l-1}(U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s})\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|U_{l-1}\|_{2,\infty}\|U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|.

Finally, because
\widetilde{U}_{l:s}
is orthogonal to
\widetilde{U}_{l-1}
, we obtain

\|U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|=\|U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|\leq\|U_{l-1}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{l-1}^{\perp}}\|\|\widetilde{U}_{l:s}\|=\|\sin\angle(U_{l-1},\widetilde{U}_{l-1})\|.

Substituting these bounds into (
F.3
) finishes the proof.

Appendix G
Extension to rectangular matrices: singular subspace perturbations

We briefly discuss the extension of current results to the rectangular model:

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}=X+E\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}},

where
X=U\Sigma V^{\mathrm{T}}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}
is a rank
r
matrix with singular values
\sigma_{1}\geq\sigma_{2}\geq\dots\geq\sigma_{r}>0
. The random noise matrix
E
has i.i.d. entries with
\mathbb{E}(E_{ij})=0
and
\mathbb{E}(E_{ij}^{2})=\sigma_{ij}^{2}.

Denote
\Sigma=(\sigma_{ij}^{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}.
The entries of
E
are sub-Gaussian with a variance profile:
\|E_{ij}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K\sigma_{ij}.

Using the standard Hermitian dilation (or linearization), we embed the rectangular matrices into larger symmetric block matrices by considering the
(n_{1}+n_{2})\times(n_{1}+n_{2})
augmented symmetric matrices:

\mathbf{A}=\begin{pmatrix}0&X\\
X^{\mathrm{T}}&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathbf{E}=\begin{pmatrix}0&E\\
E^{\mathrm{T}}&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}.

The nonzero eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}
are

\lambda_{1}=\sigma_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{r}=\sigma_{r}>0>\lambda_{r+1}=-\sigma_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{2r}=-\sigma_{r}

with corresponding eigenvectors

w_{j}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{u_{j}}{v_{j}},\qquad w_{j+r}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\binom{u_{j}}{-v_{j}},\qquad 1\leq j\leq r.

Let
\mathbf{A}=WDW^{\mathrm{T}}
, where

W=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}U&U\\
V&-V\end{pmatrix}.

For
I\subseteq[2r]
, we denote by
W_{I}
the submatrix of
W
formed by columns
\{w_{i}:i\in I\}
. All information about the top-
k
singular subspaces
U_{k}
and
V_{k}
is encoded in the eigenspace
W_{I_{k}}
, where

I_{k}:=\{1,\dots,k\}\cup\{k+1,\dots,k+r\}.

Hence, we can directly translate our eigenspace perturbation results to singular subspaces. We now introduce the relevant parameters. Define

R_{i}^{(L)}:=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}\sigma_{ij}^{2},\qquad R_{j}^{(R)}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\sigma_{ij}^{2},

and let
\mathcal{R}_{L}:=\mathrm{diag}(R_{i}^{(L)})_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}
,
\mathcal{R}_{R}:=\mathrm{diag}(R_{i}^{(R)})_{1\leq i\leq n_{2}}
,

\mathcal{R}_{\rm aug}:=\mathrm{diag}(\mathcal{R}_{L},\mathcal{R}_{R}),\qquad R_{\max}^{\rm aug}:=\max\Bigl\{\max_{i}R_{i}^{(L)},\,\max_{j}R_{j}^{(R)}\Bigr\}.

Define
M_{\rm aug}\equiv M_{\rm aug,D}
as in (
2.1
) with the dimension factor
n_{1}+n_{2}
and set

\rho_{k}^{\rm aug}:=10M_{\rm aug}+15\frac{\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm aug})}{\sigma_{k}},

and

\delta_{k}:=\sigma_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}.

Also, we denote
\mathcal{V}=W^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}_{\rm aug}W
. Note that

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}=W_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}_{\rm aug}W_{\mathcal{I}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}_{L}U_{\mathcal{I}}+V_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{R}_{R}V_{\mathcal{I}}\right).

We define
\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\rm aug}
similar to
\mathcal{B}_{k}
(
2.2
):

\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\rm aug}:=\frac{10\sqrt{k}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\left(\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{I}}\|+8\frac{R_{\max}^{\rm aug}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm aug})\right)+4\sqrt{k}\frac{\|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{K}}\|}{\sigma_{k}^{2}}.

We can then directly translate eigenspace perturbation results from the symmetric model
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}
to the rectangular model
\widetilde{X}=X+E
using the revised parameters above and the following key relationships (see
[
83
]
for details): for any unitarily invariant norm
\vvvert\cdot\vvvert,

\displaystyle\max\{\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\vvvert,\vvvert\sin\angle(V_{k},\widetilde{V}_{k})\vvvert\}\leq\vvvert\sin\angle(W_{I},\widetilde{W}_{k})\vvvert

and

\displaystyle\max\{\|\widetilde{U}_{k}-P_{U_{k}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\|_{2,\infty},\|\widetilde{V}_{k}-P_{V_{k}}\widetilde{V}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}\}\leq\|\widetilde{W}_{I}-P_{W_{I}}\widetilde{W}_{I}\|_{2,\infty}.

Specifically, this implies that the following
2\to\infty
bound holds with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
:

\displaystyle\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}(k)}\|\widetilde{U}_{k}-U_{k}O\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\max\{\|U\|_{2,\infty},\|V\|_{2,\infty}\}\left({\mathcal{B}}_{k}^{\rm aug}+30\frac{R_{\max}^{\rm aug}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}}\right)+120\sqrt{k}\frac{M_{\rm aug}}{\sigma_{k}}.

(G.1)

The same bound also holds for
\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}(k)}\|\widetilde{V}_{k}-V_{k}O\|_{2,\infty}
.

We conclude by clarifying the nature of the coupled terms in (
G
). While the dependence on both variance profiles (
\mathcal{R}_{L}
and
\mathcal{R}_{R}
) reflects the intrinsic statistical coupling in the asymmetric noise matrix, the dependence on the maximum joint incoherence
\max\{\|U\|_{2,\infty},\|V\|_{2,\infty}\}
and the total dimension
n_{1}+n_{2}
is an artifact of the symmetric embedding framework. We plan to address this limitation in future work.

Appendix H
Proofs of Lemma
3.1
, Proposition
3.1
, (
I.4
) and (
I
)

H.1
Proof of Lemma
3.1

For brevity, write

L_{\mathcal{J},s}:=I-U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}.

For
j\in\mathcal{J}
, set

\alpha_{j}(z):=\lambda_{j}^{-1}-u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)u_{j}.

Recall the quantity defined in Proposition
7.2

\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}):=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\min_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\left|1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j}\right|=\min_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\left|\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\lambda_{j}\alpha_{j}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\right|.

We first relate
s_{\min}(L_{{\mathcal{J}},s})
to
\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})
.
Decompose

U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}=\mathrm{diag}\bigl(u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j}\bigr)_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}+Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}),

where

Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}):=\mathrm{Off}\bigl(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\bigr).

Then

L_{{\mathcal{J}},s}=\mathrm{diag}\left(1-\lambda_{j}u_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})u_{j}\right)_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}-Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}.

Hence

s_{\min}(L_{{\mathcal{J}},s})\geq\frac{\Delta_{\mathcal{J}}^{\Phi}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}-\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|.

(H.1)

We next estimate the off-diagonal term
\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|
. Using the expansion

\Phi(z)=\frac{1}{z}I+\frac{1}{z^{3}}R+\varepsilon(z),

we have

Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})=\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\mathrm{Off}(\mathcal{V}_{{\mathcal{J}}{\mathcal{J}}})+\mathrm{Off}\bigl(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\bigr).

Since the eigenvalues in
{\mathcal{J}}
are positive and bounded above by

\lambda_{k+1}
,

\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|\leq\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\|\mathrm{Off}(\mathcal{V}_{{\mathcal{J}}{\mathcal{J}}})\|+\lambda_{k+1}\left\|\mathrm{Off}\bigl(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\bigr)\right\|.

By Lemma
5.2
,

\left\|\mathrm{Off}\bigl(U_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{T}}\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})U_{\mathcal{J}}\bigr)\right\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{osc}(\varepsilon(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}))\leq\frac{15}{2}\frac{R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

Therefore

\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|\leq\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{3}}\|\mathrm{Off}(V_{{\mathcal{J}}{\mathcal{J}}})\|+\frac{15}{2}\frac{\lambda_{k+1}R_{\max}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{5}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}).

(H.2)

We now verify that the off-diagonal term in (
H.1
) is
small compared with the diagonal part.
Using
\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k}
,
R_{\max}\leq 36\lambda_{k}^{2}
, and

\|\mathrm{Off}(\mathcal{V}_{{\mathcal{J}}{\mathcal{J}}})\|\leq\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R}),

from (
H.2
), we get

\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\|Q_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}\|\leq\left(8+\frac{5}{3}\right)\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}\mathrm{osc}(\mathcal{R})\leq\frac{1}{20}\left(8+\frac{5}{3}\right)\delta_{k},
